# Vendors Domain

## Overview

A Vendor in VerifyWise is a third-party service provider or supplier that an organization uses. Vendors are tracked within projects to monitor risks, compliance, and operational dependencies. The system supports vendor assessment, risk tracking, and change history auditing.

A vendor can also be researched on the open web by an agent. What it finds becomes a list of candidate risks for the vendor risk register, and nothing is written to the register until a person accepts a candidate (see [Vendor Research Agent](#vendor-research-agent)). The research also looks the vendor up in local copies of official lists (the vendor risk database, always on), and every candidate carries a credibility label computed from its sources (see [Vendor risk database](#vendor-risk-database) and [Credibility](#credibility)).

## Database Schema

### Vendors Table

```
vendors
├── id (PK)
├── order_no (INTEGER, optional)
├── vendor_name
├── vendor_provides
├── assignee (FK → users)
├── website
├── vendor_contact_person
├── review_result
├── review_status (ENUM)
├── reviewer (FK → users)
├── review_date
│
├── data_sensitivity (ENUM)
├── business_criticality (ENUM)
├── past_issues (ENUM)
├── regulatory_exposure (ENUM)
├── risk_score (INTEGER)
│
├── other_names (TEXT, optional)
├── country (CHAR(2), optional)
├── registration_number (VARCHAR(64), optional)
├── lei (CHAR(20), optional)
├── sec_cik (CHAR(10), optional)
│
├── is_demo
├── created_at
└── updated_at
```

### Vendor Projects Table

```
vendor_projects (junction table)
├── vendor_id (PK, FK → vendors)
├── project_id (PK, FK → projects)
└── is_demo
```

### Vendor Risks Table

```
vendor_risks
├── id (PK)
├── vendor_id (FK → vendors)
├── order_no
├── risk_description
├── impact_description
├── impact (ENUM)
├── likelihood (ENUM)
├── risk_severity (ENUM)
├── action_plan
├── action_owner (FK → users)
├── risk_level
├── is_demo
├── is_deleted
├── deleted_at
├── created_at
└── updated_at
```

### Vendor Research Runs Table

One row per research run of a vendor. Migration `20261010012217-create-vendor-research-tables.js`; `20261013021400-vendor-research-web-agent.js` dropped `search_provider` and added `pages_read` when the search provider was replaced by the web research agent; `20261027133032-vendor-research-run-trace.js` added `trace`; `20261027162218-vendor-research-run-progress.js` added `progress`; `20261028225356-vendor-research-scheduled-scan.js` added the trigger `scheduled` to the CHECK constraint; `20261030223852-vendor-finding-credibility.js` added `coverage` and `registry_version`; `20261101033134-vendor-research-search-engine.js` added `search_engine`.

```
vendor_research_runs
├── id (PK)
├── organization_id (FK → organizations, ON DELETE CASCADE)
├── vendor_id (FK → vendors, ON DELETE CASCADE)
├── trigger_type (VARCHAR(10): auto | manual | scheduled)
├── status (VARCHAR(10): queued | running | completed | failed, default queued)
├── categories (TEXT[], default empty)
├── category_summary (JSONB, default [])
├── evidence (JSONB, nullable)
├── model (VARCHAR(255), nullable)
├── prompt_version (VARCHAR(255), nullable)
├── searches_used (INTEGER, default 0)
├── pages_read (INTEGER, default 0)
├── duplicates_dropped (INTEGER, default 0)
├── error (TEXT, nullable)
├── trace (JSONB, nullable)
├── progress (JSONB, nullable)
├── coverage (JSONB, nullable)
├── registry_version (VARCHAR(10), nullable)
├── search_engine (VARCHAR(16): anthropic | openai | gdelt, nullable)
├── requested_by (FK → users, ON DELETE SET NULL)
├── created_at (TIMESTAMPTZ, default now)
├── started_at (TIMESTAMPTZ, nullable)
└── finished_at (TIMESTAMPTZ, nullable)
```

All columns are NOT NULL except the ones marked nullable and `requested_by`. `trigger_type` and `status` carry CHECK constraints.

| Index | Columns | Purpose |
|-------|---------|---------|
| `vendor_research_runs_active_uniq` (partial unique) | `(organization_id, vendor_id) WHERE status IN ('queued', 'running')` | A vendor has at most one active run, so two clicks create one run |
| `idx_vendor_research_runs_vendor_created` | `(organization_id, vendor_id, created_at DESC)` | Latest run of a vendor, cooldown |
| `idx_vendor_research_runs_org_created` | `(organization_id, created_at)` | Daily run cap |
| `idx_vendor_research_runs_vendor` | `(vendor_id)` | The cascade when a vendor is deleted. The three indexes above all start with `organization_id`, so they cannot serve a lookup on `vendor_id` alone |

Column notes:

- `category_summary` is an array of `{ category, outcome, note }`; `outcome` is `findings`, `nothing_found`, `search_failed` or `skipped`, and `note` is one of a closed set of phrases (see [Category outcomes](#category-outcomes)). It is empty until the run ends, and for most failed runs.
- `categories` lists the categories that the research worked on: those with at least one call that reached the web (a call that the loop's own time limit cut off is not recorded and does not count).
- `evidence` is the evidence pack the analysis was shown (sources with `id`, `category`, `kind` (`result` or `page`), `onVendorSite`, `url`, `title`, `content`, `publishedAt`). It is an **audit trail** of third-party text: no endpoint returns it. The 100,000-byte cap is applied in code, not by the DDL.
- `model` (the id of the model that was called; `<research model> / <analysis model>` when an Admin chose another key for the analysis) and `prompt_version` (`CREDIBILITY_PROMPT_VERSION`, now `vr-15`; a run from before the credibility rules holds `vr-6`, and one from the milestones in between `vr-10`, `vr-11`, `vr-12`, `vr-13` or `vr-14`) record what produced the run. `searches_used` counts the `search` actions that reached the search engine, a failed one included; `pages_read` counts the pages that were read. Both are returned by the API. `search_engine` is the engine the run's searches went to (`anthropic`: Claude's web search, `gdelt`: the news of the GDELT Project; the CHECK constraint also allows `openai`, which no run stores). It is recorded only when at least one search was answered: a run whose searches the web never answered received no news, so the screen does not credit an engine for it (the log line of the research loop keeps the engine and the count of answered searches). It is null for a run from before the column, for a run that searched nothing and for a run that has not ended. `coverage` (`RunCoverage`) says which lists and databases the run checked and with what outcome, and `registry_version` which version of the [source registry](#credibility) classified its sources; both are null for a run from before the credibility rules and for a run that ended before they were written.
- `error` is set when the run failed and is always one of a closed set of fixed English phrases, cut to 500 characters in code. Raw exception messages go to the server log only.
- `started_at` is set only when the job moves the run to `running`. A `failed` run with a null `started_at` never started, which matters for the limits below.
- `requested_by` is the user of a manual run, and null for an automatic or a scheduled run or when that user was deleted.
- `progress` is where a **running** run is, for the page that follows it: `{ phase, steps, action, category, searchesUsed, pagesRead, categories, updatedAt }`. `phase` is `researching` or `analysing`; `steps` is the number of model turns the loop has taken; `action` and `category` are those of the latest turn (`search` or `read`, and one of the six categories, or null); the counts and `categories` (the categories worked on so far, in the order of the categories) are those of the web session; `updatedAt` is when it was written, so that a reader can tell whether the run has moved. It holds counts, closed words and a time and **nothing that a web page or the model wrote** (no query, address, title or thought: those are in the trace, for administrators), which is why it is part of the run that every role reads. It is written at every turn of the loop and when the analysis starts, and it is null for a queued run and for every run that has ended: the queries that end a run (`finishRunQuery`, `failStaleRunsQuery`) empty it. See [Live progress](#live-progress).
- `trace` is the record of what the run did: its steps, model calls with their tokens, the candidates that were turned away and why, and the time of each phase (see the [run trace](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-run-trace)). It is at most 200,000 bytes, it holds queries and addresses, and it is returned to administrators only, by its own endpoint; the run in every other response never carries it. It is null for a run from before the column and for a run that failed before it started.

### Vendor Risk Findings Table

One row per candidate risk that a run produced. A finding is a candidate for the vendor risk register. The register table is `vendorrisks` (the Vendor Risks Table above calls it `vendor_risks` and lists an `impact` column that the real table does not have). A finding stays `pending` until a person accepts or dismisses it.

```
vendor_risk_findings
├── id (PK)
├── organization_id (FK → organizations, ON DELETE CASCADE)
├── vendor_id (FK → vendors, ON DELETE CASCADE)
├── run_id (FK → vendor_research_runs, ON DELETE CASCADE)
├── category (VARCHAR(30): security_incidents | certifications | legal_regulatory | ai_specific | availability | financial_health)
├── risk_description (TEXT)
├── impact_description (TEXT)
├── action_plan (TEXT)
├── likelihood (ENUM enum_vendorrisks_likelihood, the register's type)
├── risk_severity (ENUM enum_vendorrisks_risk_severity, the register's type)
├── risk_level (VARCHAR(255), for example "High Risk")
├── confidence (VARCHAR(10): low | medium | high, nullable)
├── credibility (VARCHAR(10): low | medium | high, nullable)
├── credibility_basis (JSONB, nullable)
├── rationale (TEXT, nullable)
├── sources (JSONB)
├── snippet_only (BOOLEAN, default false)
├── status (VARCHAR(10): pending | accepted | dismissed, default pending)
├── dismissed_reason (VARCHAR(20): not_applicable | already_covered | inaccurate | accepted_risk | wrong_vendor | outdated | other, nullable)
├── dismissed_note (TEXT, nullable)
├── accepted_vendor_risk_id (FK → vendorrisks, ON DELETE SET NULL)
├── decided_by (FK → users, ON DELETE SET NULL)
├── decided_at (TIMESTAMPTZ, nullable)
└── created_at (TIMESTAMPTZ, default now)
```

All columns are NOT NULL except `confidence`, `credibility`, `credibility_basis`, `rationale`, `dismissed_reason`, `dismissed_note`, `accepted_vendor_risk_id`, `decided_by` and `decided_at`. `credibility` and `credibility_basis` are the label of a finding and why it has it (see [Credibility](#credibility)): every run sets them. A finding of a run from before the credibility rules has only its `confidence`, and the reads give it that level as its label on the basis `{ "rule": "legacy_confidence" }` until the backfill gives it its own (see [Backfill of the label (M3)](#backfill-of-the-label-m3)). No run writes `confidence` any more: it lost its NOT NULL in `20261030223852-vendor-finding-credibility.js`, and the column stays, read-only, until the contract migration that drops it (the follow-up branch `feature/vendor-finding-drop-confidence`, for the release after this one). `rationale` is the one sentence in which the model says why the likelihood and the severity are what they are; it is null for the findings of runs from before it existed, and it is plain text that the page shows and does not copy into the register. `snippet_only` is true when the sources of the finding are search results that no page confirmed (see [The check of a finding that rests on snippets](#the-check-of-a-finding-that-rests-on-snippets)); it is false for the findings of runs from before the column, for a finding that rests on a page and for one that was checked and confirmed. `category`, `confidence`, `credibility`, `status` and `dismissed_reason` carry CHECK constraints (the one on `category` allows the six categories: one migration widened it for `availability` and `financial_health` together).

| Index | Columns | Purpose |
|-------|---------|---------|
| `idx_vendor_risk_findings_org_status_created` | `(organization_id, status, created_at DESC)` | The findings list |
| `idx_vendor_risk_findings_org_vendor` | `(organization_id, vendor_id)` | Findings of one vendor, de-duplication |
| `idx_vendor_risk_findings_run` | `(run_id)` | The cascade when a run is deleted |
| `idx_vendor_risk_findings_vendor` | `(vendor_id)` | The cascade when a vendor is deleted. The indexes above all start with `organization_id`, so they cannot serve a lookup on `vendor_id` alone |
| `idx_vendor_risk_findings_accepted_risk` (partial) | `(accepted_vendor_risk_id) WHERE accepted_vendor_risk_id IS NOT NULL` | The `SET NULL` when a register risk is deleted. Most findings are never accepted, so only the rows that point at a risk are indexed |

The last three indexes of this table, and `idx_vendor_research_runs_vendor` of the runs table, come from their own migration, `20261011025519-vendor-research-fk-indexes.js` (`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, reversible). Without an index on the child column, each foreign-key action scans the whole table, and both tables hold the rows of every tenant. `vendor_risk_findings.decided_by` and `vendor_research_runs.requested_by` get none: deleting a user is rare.

Column notes:

- `sources` is an array of `{ url, title, quote, published_at }` with at least one entry. The quote is a verbatim part of the text of that source (a snippet or a page excerpt), at most 300 characters.
- The three text columns are at most 300 characters each. That limit, and the rule that a finding has at least one source, are enforced in code (the output schema and the validation), not by the DDL. The vendor risk form takes up to 500 characters for the risk and the impact and 1,000 for the action plan (the limits of the server, `VENDOR_RISK_VALIDATION_LIMITS`), so every accepted finding can be edited in the register.
- `risk_level` is `calculateRiskLevel(risk_severity, likelihood)` followed by ` Risk`, as in the register.
- There is no unique constraint on the wording: the model words a risk differently on every run, so de-duplication is done in code (see [De-duplication](#de-duplication)).
- Accepting a finding keeps its row and sets `accepted_vendor_risk_id` to the new register row, `decided_by` and `decided_at`. Dismissing sets `dismissed_reason`, `dismissed_note`, `decided_by` and `decided_at`.
- `wrong_vendor` (the sources are about another company) and `outdated` (the sources are out of date) were added by `20261027133608-vendor-finding-dismiss-reasons.js`, which replaces the CHECK constraint of `dismissed_reason`. They tell a miss of the search apart from a risk the person does not care about, which is what the [decision statistics](#vendor-research-endpoints) are read for.

### Vendor Research Settings Table

Which of the organization's LLM keys the research uses, and how often the scheduled scan researches its vendors, as an Admin chose. One row per organization, written when an Admin first chooses. Migration `20261027155525-vendor-research-settings.js`; `20261028225356-vendor-research-scheduled-scan.js` added `scan_interval_days`.

```
vendor_research_settings
├── organization_id (PK, FK → organizations, ON DELETE CASCADE)
├── research_llm_key_id (FK → llm_keys, ON DELETE SET NULL, nullable)
├── analysis_llm_key_id (FK → llm_keys, ON DELETE SET NULL, nullable)
├── scan_interval_days (SMALLINT, nullable, CHECK 1 to 365)
├── updated_by (FK → users, ON DELETE SET NULL, nullable)
└── updated_at (TIMESTAMPTZ, default now)
```

A `NULL` key is a choice: "the default". The default for the research is the newest key of the organization, and for the analysis the key of the research, which is what every run did before the choice existed, so an organization that never chooses sees no change. Deleting a key empties a choice that named it (the foreign key does it), so a key that is gone is the default again; the code also treats an id that is not in the organization's key list as the default, in case a read is stale. See [Which key a run uses](#which-key-a-run-uses).

`scan_interval_days` is the number of days the scheduled scan lets pass before it researches a vendor of the organization again. `NULL` is off, and it is what every organization has until an Admin chooses. The API takes 7, 14 and 30; the column takes any whole number of days up to a year, so that a new choice is not a migration. See [The scheduled scan](#the-scheduled-scan).

### Vendor Research Sources Table

The sources that a completed run showed to the model, one row for each document of a vendor, so that a scheduled run can analyse the sources that are new and leave the others (see [The memory of the sources](#the-memory-of-the-sources)). Migration `20261028225356-vendor-research-scheduled-scan.js`.

```
vendor_research_sources
├── id (PK)
├── organization_id (FK → organizations, ON DELETE CASCADE)
├── vendor_id (FK → vendors, ON DELETE CASCADE)
├── source_key (TEXT)
├── first_seen_run_id (FK → vendor_research_runs, ON DELETE SET NULL, nullable)
├── first_seen_at (TIMESTAMPTZ, default now)
└── last_seen_at (TIMESTAMPTZ, default now)
```

`source_key` is the identity of a document, made by `sourceKey` in `services/webResearch/urlCanon.ts` from the address alone: the site (without `www.`), the path without the marks that name the same document in other forms, and the query string. `(organization_id, vendor_id, source_key)` is unique, and the unique index leads with `organization_id`, so it serves the cascade from a deleted organization and the lookups of one vendor. The two other indexes (`vendor_id`, `first_seen_run_id`) serve the cascade from a deleted vendor and the `SET NULL` of a deleted run, which forgets which run saw a source first and keeps the row.

### Vendor Research Snapshots Table

The pages where a vendor states its terms (its terms, privacy policy, data processing addendum, list of subprocessors and security page), as the last completed run that read them saw them: one row for each document of a vendor, so that the next run can tell what changed on a page (see [Policy page changes](#policy-page-changes)). Migration `20261028232304-vendor-research-snapshots.js`.

```
vendor_research_snapshots
├── id (PK)
├── organization_id (FK → organizations, ON DELETE CASCADE)
├── vendor_id (FK → vendors, ON DELETE CASCADE)
├── page_key (TEXT)
├── url (TEXT)
├── kind (VARCHAR(20), CHECK in terms, privacy, dpa, subprocessors, security)
├── title (TEXT, default '')
├── text_hash (VARCHAR(64))
├── body (TEXT)
├── captured_run_id (FK → vendor_research_runs, ON DELETE SET NULL, nullable)
├── first_seen_at (TIMESTAMPTZ, default now)
├── captured_at (TIMESTAMPTZ, default now)
└── last_seen_at (TIMESTAMPTZ, default now)
```

`page_key` is the identity of the document, made by `sourceKey` like the key of the sources. `body` is the text of the page as the code keeps it, line by line (white space squeezed, at most 4,000 lines and 100,000 characters, a line cut at 600), and `text_hash` is the SHA-256 of those lines; the limits are the code's and not the DDL's. `captured_at` and `captured_run_id` move only when the text changed, `last_seen_at` moves with every completed run that reads the page, and `first_seen_at` is the first one that did. `(organization_id, vendor_id, page_key)` is unique, and the unique index leads with `organization_id`, so it serves the cascade from a deleted organization and the lookups of one vendor. The two other indexes (`vendor_id`, `captured_run_id`) serve the cascade from a deleted vendor and the `SET NULL` of a deleted run, which forgets which run captured a text and keeps the row. A page that no completed run has read for 365 days is deleted when the next run of its vendor writes its pages.

The five tables are tenant-scoped. They are registered in `tests/integration/tenant-isolation/tenantIsolation.registry.ts` with isolation tests.

## Enumerations

### Review Status

```typescript
enum ReviewStatus {
  NOT_STARTED = "Not started"
  IN_REVIEW = "In review"
  REVIEWED = "Reviewed"
  REQUIRES_FOLLOWUP = "Requires follow-up"
}
```

The review status and the review date are optional, and a vendor stored by an import or the API may have neither (the list of vendors shows "No review date"). A create without them gets "Not started" and the day. `PATCH /vendors/:id` (or `/v1/vendors/:id`) with an empty text or `null` for either clears it: the database takes no empty text for an enum or a timestamp, which used to fail the update with a 500. The vendor form sends `null` for a status or date the person left empty, and shows no review date for a vendor without one. Any other status, on a create or an update, is a 400 whose sentence names the field and its values ("Review status must be one of: Not started, In review, Reviewed, Requires follow-up"), and so is a review date that JavaScript's `Date` cannot read, such as `"2026-13-45"` or an object ("Review date must be a valid date"); both used to fail with a 500 from the database. A number is a date in milliseconds since 1970.

### Data Sensitivity

```typescript
enum DataSensitivity {
  NONE = "None"
  INTERNAL_ONLY = "Internal only"
  PII = "Personally identifiable information (PII)"
  FINANCIAL = "Financial data"
  HEALTH = "Health data (e.g. HIPAA)"
  MODEL_WEIGHTS = "Model weights or AI assets"
  OTHER = "Other sensitive data"
}
```

### Business Criticality

```typescript
enum BusinessCriticality {
  LOW = "Low (vendor supports non-core functions)"
  MEDIUM = "Medium (affects operations but is replaceable)"
  HIGH = "High (critical to core services or products)"
}
```

### Past Issues

```typescript
enum PastIssues {
  NONE = "None"
  MINOR = "Minor incident (e.g. small delay, minor bug)"
  MAJOR = "Major incident (e.g. data breach, legal issue)"
}
```

### Regulatory Exposure

```typescript
enum RegulatoryExposure {
  NONE = "None"
  GDPR = "GDPR (EU)"
  HIPAA = "HIPAA (US)"
  SOC2 = "SOC 2"
  ISO27001 = "ISO 27001"
  EU_AI_ACT = "EU AI act"
  CCPA = "CCPA (california)"
  OTHER = "Other"
}
```

A scorecard answer is one of these values, written exactly so (the lists are `VENDOR_DATA_SENSITIVITY_ENUM` and its neighbours in `utils/validations/vendorValidation.utils.ts`). Any other value, on a create or an update, is a 400 whose sentence names the field and its values ("Data sensitivity must be one of: None, Internal only, …"), where the database used to refuse it with a 500. The model checks the review status, the review date and the answers before anything is written (`checkListsAndDate` in `domain.layer/models/vendor/vendor.model.ts`); the sentences are translated in `Servers/locales/{en,de,fr}.json`. `PATCH /vendors/:id` (or `/v1/vendors/:id`) with an empty text or `null` for an answer clears it, as for the review status, and a create takes an empty answer as none. The vendor form sends nothing for an answer left empty, so a save from the form keeps the stored answer. The AI advisor's vendor tools write without the model, but their definitions offer only these values.

### Risk Impact

```typescript
enum Impact {
  NEGLIGIBLE = "Negligible"
  MINOR = "Minor"
  MODERATE = "Moderate"
  MAJOR = "Major"
  CRITICAL = "Critical"
}
```

### Risk Likelihood

```typescript
enum Likelihood {
  RARE = "Rare"
  UNLIKELY = "Unlikely"
  POSSIBLE = "Possible"
  LIKELY = "Likely"
  ALMOST_CERTAIN = "Almost certain"
}
```

## API Endpoints

### Vendor Endpoints

| Method | Endpoint | Description |
|--------|----------|-------------|
| GET | `/vendors/` | Get all vendors |
| GET | `/vendors/:id` | Get vendor by ID |
| GET | `/vendors/project-id/:id` | Get vendors by project |
| GET | `/vendors/:id/riskSuggestions` | Read-only vendor risk suggestions derived from the questionnaire |
| POST | `/vendors/` | Create vendor (develop's contract: it ignores `other_names`, `country`, `registration_number`, `lei` and `sec_cik`) |
| PATCH | `/vendors/:id` | Update vendor (develop's contract: it ignores the same five fields) |
| DELETE | `/vendors/:id` | Delete vendor |
| POST | `/v1/vendors` | Create a vendor with its other names and identifiers (`vendor.edit`) |
| PATCH | `/v1/vendors/:id` | Update a vendor, with its other names and identifiers (`vendor.edit`) |

The create and the update of a vendor that take the **other names** and the **identifiers** (`other_names`, `country`, `registration_number`, `lei` and `sec_cik`) are served under `/api/v1` (ADR 0001 sections 2 and 5): develop's contract did not have these request fields, and a changed contract goes to `/api/v1`. `POST /v1/vendors` and `PATCH /v1/vendors/:id` are thin adapters over the same controller code as the legacy writes (`createVendorV1` and `updateVendorByIdV1` in `controllers/vendor.ctrl.ts`), with the same permission (`vendor.edit`), validation, statuses, change history, notifications and automatic research, and the client calls them. The legacy `POST /vendors` and `PATCH /vendors/:id` keep develop's contract: they drop the five fields at the entry (`withoutV1OnlyVendorFields` in `utils/validations/vendorValidation.utils.ts`), so they neither validate, write nor record them in the change history, and a legacy call that sends them is answered as if it had not. The reads and the delete did not change, so they have no `/v1` copy (ADR 0001 section 5): they stay on `/vendors` and return the five columns, which are nullable.

The research of a vendor (start a run, the latest run, the trace of a run) is served under `/api/v1/vendors/:id/research`, not here: see [Vendor Research Endpoints](#vendor-research-endpoints).

### Vendor Risk Endpoints

| Method | Endpoint | Description |
|--------|----------|-------------|
| GET | `/vendorRisks/all` | Get all risks |
| GET | `/vendorRisks/by-projid/:id` | Get by project |
| GET | `/vendorRisks/by-vendorid/:id` | Get by vendor |
| GET | `/vendorRisks/:id` | Get risk by ID |
| POST | `/vendorRisks/` | Create risk |
| PATCH | `/vendorRisks/:id` | Update risk |
| DELETE | `/vendorRisks/:id` | Soft delete risk |

### Change History Endpoints

| Method | Endpoint | Description |
|--------|----------|-------------|
| GET | `/vendor-change-history/:id` | Vendor changes |
| GET | `/vendor-risk-change-history/:id` | Risk changes |

### Vendor Research Endpoints

The endpoints are served under `/api/v1` (ADR 0001: the research agent was added after the versioning policy), so the paths below, which are relative to `/api`, start with `/v1`. The vendor reads and the delete stay on the legacy `/api/vendors`, and the create and the update of a vendor are served at `/api/v1/vendors` too (see [Vendor Endpoints](#vendor-endpoints)). A success body is `{ message, data }`. Every route needs a signed-in user; starting research and deciding a finding also need the `vendor.edit` permission, the key of the writes to vendors and vendor risks (`POST /vendors`, `POST /vendorRisks`; Admin and Editor by default, custom roles as configured), so Reviewers and Auditors can read but get 403 on the three writes. The two routes about how the agent performs and the two routes of its settings need `llmKeys.admin` (the built-in Admin role): the first show queries, addresses and the way people decided, and the settings choose which LLM key the organization's data goes to, which the other roles do not need.

| Method | Endpoint | Access | Request | Success |
|--------|----------|--------|---------|---------|
| GET | `/v1/vendor-research/config` | Any signed-in user | none | 200, `{ hasLlmKey, autoRunEnabled }` |
| GET | `/v1/vendor-risk-findings` | Any signed-in user | query: `status`, `vendorId`, `limit`, `offset` | 200, `{ rows, total }` |
| POST | `/v1/vendors/:id/research` | `vendor.edit` | none | 202, `{ run, alreadyRunning }` |
| GET | `/v1/vendors/:id/research/latest` | Any signed-in user | none | 200, the run, or `null` |
| POST | `/v1/vendor-risk-findings/:id/accept` | `vendor.edit` | body, all optional: `action_owner`, and the edits `risk_description`, `impact_description`, `action_plan`, `likelihood`, `risk_severity`, `risk_level`, `frameworks` | 200, `{ finding, vendorRisk }` |
| POST | `/v1/vendor-risk-findings/:id/dismiss` | `vendor.edit` | body: `reason`, `note` (optional) | 200, the finding |
| GET | `/v1/vendors/:id/research/runs/:runId/trace` | `llmKeys.admin` | none | 200, the trace of the run |
| GET | `/v1/vendor-research/decision-stats` | `llmKeys.admin` | none | 200, `{ byPromptVersion, byCategory }` |
| GET | `/v1/vendor-research/settings` | `llmKeys.admin` | none | 200, `{ researchKeyId, analysisKeyId, scanIntervalDays }` (`null` where nothing is chosen) |
| PUT | `/v1/vendor-research/settings` | `llmKeys.admin` | body: `researchKeyId` and `analysisKeyId`, each a positive whole number or `null`; `scanIntervalDays` (optional), 7, 14, 30 or `null` | 200, what was kept, in the same shape |

**Config.** Booleans only, never a key. `hasLlmKey` is true when the organization has at least one LLM key, which is all that research needs to be set up; `autoRunEnabled` reflects `VENDOR_RESEARCH_AUTO_RUN`.

**Findings list.** `status` is `pending` (the default), `accepted` or `dismissed`. `vendorId` narrows to one vendor; a vendor that does not exist in the organization gives an empty list, not a 404. `limit` defaults to 50 and a larger value is cut to 200; `offset` defaults to 0. A bad `status`, `vendorId`, `limit` (below 1 or not a whole number) or `offset`, or a parameter given twice, is a 400. Each row is the full finding row plus `vendor_name`. The newest run comes first, and inside a run the most severe finding comes first.

**Start.** The checks run in this order and the first that fails answers: the vendor exists in the organization (404), the organization has an LLM key (409 `llm_key_missing`). There is no check for a search setting: the search needs no account. Then the vendor's stuck runs are failed and, if the vendor has an active run, that run is returned instead of a new one (still 202, with `alreadyRunning: true`, so a click during a run is never a 429). Then, for a manual start only, the cooldown (429 `cooldown`), and for every start the daily cap (429 `daily_cap`). Only then is the queued run created and its job queued. The start waits at most 5 seconds (`ENQUEUE_TIMEOUT_MS`) for that: with Redis down the queue does not refuse the job, it keeps waiting. If the job cannot be queued in that time, the run is failed at once (`could not enqueue`, which counts toward neither limit) and the answer is 503 `enqueue_failed`. A job that is queued after that is skipped by the worker, without a web request or a model call. (A job that lands in the few milliseconds before the run is marked failed could still start it, and the vendor is then researched twice at worst.)

**Latest.** The run of the vendor with the newest `created_at`, whatever its status, or `null` for a vendor that was never researched. This read also writes: before it reads, it fails the vendor's runs that have been queued for more than 15 minutes or running for more than 25 (the run's own 20-minute limit plus 5 minutes of grace, see [Limits](#limits)), so a lost job never shows as "researching" for ever.

**Accept.** `action_owner` absent means the vendor's assignee, but only when that person is a user of the organization (otherwise nobody); `null` means nobody; a number is that user, who must belong to the organization (400 if not). It must be a JSON number: a string such as `"5"` is a 400.

The other fields are **edits**: what the person changed in the vendor risk form that was opened on the finding (see [Findings section](#vendor-research)). A field that is absent keeps the finding's value, so an empty body accepts the finding as it is. They are checked before anything is locked or written, and the first one that is not valid is a 400 whose sentence names it ("The risk description must be 1 to 500 characters", "Invalid likelihood", ...):

| Field | Valid |
|-------|-------|
| `risk_description`, `impact_description` | A string of 1 to 500 characters (code points) once control characters are removed and the ends are trimmed |
| `action_plan` | The same, 1 to 1,000 characters |
| `likelihood`, `risk_severity` | One of the register's own values (`VENDOR_RISK_ENUMS`; note the top likelihood is `Almost certain` with a small c) |
| `risk_level` | A string of 1 to 50 characters. It is stored as it is sent, as the form's own save does, because the form shows the level it works out and saves that one |
| `frameworks` | An array of at most 100 positive whole numbers; duplicates are dropped. Empty means none |

The vendor is always the finding's own: `vendor_id`, and everything else in the body that is not listed, is ignored. When `risk_level` is absent and the likelihood or the severity was changed, the level is worked out from the pair the way the research stores it (`<Level> Risk`); when neither changed, it is the finding's.

**Dismiss.** `reason` is one of `not_applicable`, `already_covered`, `inaccurate`, `accepted_risk`, `wrong_vendor`, `outdated`, `other`. `note` is optional; `null`, an empty note and a note of blanks all mean no note. The note is cleaned of control characters, trimmed, and then counted: at most 500 characters (code points), else 400.

**Trace.** The trace of one run of the vendor (see the [run trace](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-run-trace)), as stored: `{ version, steps, modelCalls, rejected, dropped, phases, totals, stoppedBecause, truncated }`. A bad vendor or run id is a 400, and a vendor or run that is not in the caller's organization, a run of another vendor and a run without a trace are all the same 404 ("Research trace not found"). Reading the trace changes nothing.

**Settings.** Which LLM key the research uses (see [Which key a run uses](#which-key-a-run-uses)) and how often the scheduled scan researches the vendors (see [The scheduled scan](#the-scheduled-scan)). `GET` gives the choice, with `null` for a key that is not chosen, and a key that has been deleted since is `null` again (the foreign key empties it). It never returns a key, only ids. `PUT` replaces the whole choice, so both fields are required: a missing field, an id that is not a JSON number above zero (`"1"`, `0`, `1.5`) or a body that is not an object is a 400, and writes nothing. An id that is not a key of the caller's organization, a key of another organization included, is a 404 (the same answer as a key that does not exist), and the choice that was saved before stays. A choice of two `null`s is how an Admin gives the default back. `scanIntervalDays` is 7, 14, 30 or `null` (off); any other value, a string included, is a 400. A body without it leaves the interval as it is, so a caller that knows only the keys cannot switch the scan off by mistake, and `null` switches it off. The row records who chose last (`updated_by`).

**Decision statistics.** How the findings of the organization were decided, so that a change to the agent can be judged by what people did with its findings: `byCategory` has one entry for each prompt version and category with `pending`, `accepted`, `dismissed`, `acceptanceRate` (accepted over decided, `null` while nothing is decided) and `dismissedByReason`; `byPromptVersion` adds the categories of each version up. A run from before the prompt version was recorded has `promptVersion: null`. The same decisions are counted as the OpenTelemetry counter `vendor_research.finding.decisions` (attributes `decision`, `category` and `reason`; see [Observability](../infrastructure/observability.md)).

The **run** in a response is an allowlist, not the row:

```typescript
{
  id, vendor_id,
  status: "queued" | "running" | "completed" | "failed",
  trigger_type: "auto" | "manual" | "scheduled",
  categories: string[],
  category_summary: { category, outcome, note }[],
  searches_used: number,
  pages_read: number,
  duplicates_dropped: number,
  error: string | null,
  progress: RunProgress | null,   // where a running run is; null otherwise (see Live progress)
  coverage: RunCoverage | null,   // what a run of the vendor risk database checked; null otherwise
  started_at: string | null,
  finished_at: string | null,
  created_at: string
}
```

The evidence pack, model, prompt version, requester, organization and trace never leave the server in this object (the trace has its own administrator endpoint); the two counters `searches_used` and `pages_read` do. A **finding** is returned as its full row, `credibility` and `credibility_basis` included, with one field added: `credibility_rationale`, the English line of its credibility label (`credibilityRationale` in `services/vendorResearch/credibilityText.ts`), or `null` for a finding without a label, for a client that does not word the line itself (see [Credibility](#credibility)). `coverage` is in the run on purpose: it holds the ids, outcomes, days and counts of what the run checked, and the names and attributions of the lists. `vendorRisk` is the JSON of the new register row, as the vendor risk form returns it.

Errors:

| Status | Body | Meaning |
|--------|------|---------|
| 400 | `{ message: "Bad Request", data: "<sentence>" }` | A bad id, query parameter or body |
| 403 | `{ message: "Forbidden", data: "Access denied" }` | The role lacks `vendor.edit` (`llmKeys.admin` for the trace, the decision statistics and the settings) |
| 404 | `{ message: "Not Found", data: "<sentence>" }` | No such vendor, finding or (for the settings) LLM key in the caller's organization. One that belongs to another organization is answered the same way |
| 409 on start | `{ message: "Conflict", data: { code } }` | `code` is `llm_key_missing`: the organization has no LLM key |
| 409 on accept | `{ message: "Conflict", data: "<sentence>" }` | The finding is no longer pending, or another decision on it is being made at this moment. Accept does not wait for that decision, so a retry can succeed if it let go without deciding |
| 409 on dismiss | `{ message: "Conflict", data: "<sentence>" }` | The finding is no longer pending. A dismissal that meets a decision in progress waits for it: it answers 409 if that decision went through, and dismisses the finding if it did not |
| 429 on start | `{ message: "Too Many Requests", data: { code: "cooldown", retryAfterSeconds } }` and a `Retry-After` header, or `data: { code: "daily_cap" }` | A rate limit (see [Limits](#limits)) |
| 503 on start | `{ message: "Service Unavailable", error: "<sentence>" }` | The job queue is unavailable. There is no `data` and no code, so tell it by the status. Trying again is safe: the failed run counts for neither limit |
| 500 | `{ message: "Internal Server Error", error: "<message>" }` | Server error |

## Create Vendor

### Request

```typescript
POST /v1/vendors
{
  vendor_name: string,
  vendor_provides: string,
  assignee: number,
  website: string,
  vendor_contact_person: string,
  review_result?: string,
  review_status?: string,
  reviewer?: number,
  review_date?: Date,
  data_sensitivity?: string,
  business_criticality?: string,
  past_issues?: string,
  regulatory_exposure?: string,
  risk_score?: number,
  other_names?: string,
  country?: string | null,
  registration_number?: string | null,
  lei?: string | null,
  sec_cik?: string | null,
  projects: number[]
}
```

`POST /vendors` takes the same body without `other_names`, `country`, `registration_number`, `lei` and `sec_cik`: it ignores them, as it did before a vendor had them.

`other_names` is an optional text of the other names the vendor is known by ("Facebook" for "Meta Platforms"), separated by commas, semicolons or line breaks. A legal form after a comma belongs to the name before it: "Acme Analytics, Inc." is one name, and so are "Hanbit Electronics Co., Ltd." and "Contoso Company, The" (`otherNamesIn`, with the legal forms of `services/vendorResearch/identity.ts`). A legal form with no name before it is a name, so a brand that is also a legal form (SAS) is written first. It is stored as a tidy list ("Facebook, Instagram"), a name written twice once. At most 500 characters, 8 names, and 2 to 80 characters in a name with a letter or a digit in it (`validateOtherNames` in `utils/validations/vendorValidation.utils.ts`, the same limits as the form). `PATCH /v1/vendors/:id` takes it too; an empty text or `null` clears it. The answer of a create or an update does not carry it (nor the scorecard columns); `GET /vendors/:id` does. The vendor research names the vendor by these names as well, see [Other names](#other-names).

`country`, `registration_number`, `lei` and `sec_cik` are the vendor's **identifiers** (spec Q1), each optional, checked and stored in one form by the server (`validateCountry`, `validateRegistrationNumber`, `validateLei`, `validateSecCik` with their `normalize…` functions in `utils/validations/vendorValidation.utils.ts`, applied by the model in `domain.layer/models/vendor/vendor.model.ts`; the vendor form checks the same rules in `Clients/src/application/validations/vendorIdentifiersValidation.ts`): the country is a two-letter code of ISO 3166-1, stored in upper case (249 codes; not `UK`, `EU` or `XK`); the registration number at most 64 characters, its white space made single; the LEI 18 letters or digits and two check digits that match (ISO 17442, MOD 97-10), stored without spaces in upper case; the SEC CIK one to ten digits, stored as ten with zeros in front. `PATCH /v1/vendors/:id` takes them too, and `null` or an empty text clears one; `GET /vendors/:id` returns them. The columns, with checks of the stored form, are in `20261031023746-vendor-identifiers.js`. What each changes in the research:

- **The country** chooses the market of the news searches (see [News in the vendor's language](#news-in-the-vendors-language)) and the authority named in the legal and regulatory news, ahead of the country domain of the website and the legal form (`marketOf`, `regulatorTopicsOf` in `services/vendorResearch/market.ts`). US, GB, IE and AU mean English with that country's authority, whatever the domain and the legal form say; a country that neither table holds (any but DE, AT, FR, IT, ES, NL, PT, BR, US, GB, IE, AU) changes nothing.
- **The registration number** ties an entry of a sanctions list that carries the same number (the UK list's business registration numbers, the EU list's identification numbers) to the vendor strongly, but only when the entry also shares a name with the vendor: a number is unique only in its own register. A number of fewer than six letters and digits is not used.
- **The LEI** ties an entry that carries it to the vendor strongly, alone.
- **The SEC CIK** is the registrant the SEC is asked about, strongly, in place of the one registrant whose name is the vendor's (`storedCikOf` in `records/sec.ts`, which reads digits only; the SEC read-through of the vendor risk database asks by it). It is also the CIK under which a cited EDGAR filing counts as the vendor's own word, when the header of the filing names it as a filer (Q28, see [Credibility](#credibility)).

### Processing

1. Validate required fields
2. Create vendor record
3. Create vendor-project associations
4. Record creation in change history
5. Trigger `vendor_added` automation

## Other names

A vendor has one name in the record, and the press may use another ("Meta Platforms" is "Facebook" in an article). A source that names the vendor only by that other name would be taken for a source about someone else, so the research and its checks know the vendor by several names (`services/vendorResearch/aliases.ts`):

- **The names a person wrote** in the vendor's **Other names** field (`vendors.other_names`), trusted whatever they are; a legal name written with a comma is one name, never a name "Inc.".
- **The names the vendor's own home page gives itself**, read from its meta tags and its JSON-LD data (`services/webResearch/siteIdentity.ts`). A page is not trusted: its names are taken only when the page names the vendor, is on the vendor's own site, and a name is built around the first word of the vendor's name or the brand of its domain. The products it lists are for the briefing of the model only, never names.

At most 8 names are kept, the ones a person wrote first. The same names are used by the matcher that keeps a result out of the evidence (`identity.ts`), by the queries (a query that names the vendor only by one of them keeps it in front; the code makes one more news search for each category, led by the first that does not give the name's search again: the name with a legal form, or the domain), by the pages it reads around the names, by the prompts (`<also_known_as>` in the vendor block) and by the analysis and its checks, so that a source the research kept is not turned away for being about someone else. The names go to the organization's LLM provider and into web searches, like the vendor's name. See [web-research](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-names-a-vendor-is-known-by).

## News in the vendor's language

The news feed answers a query in the language it is written in, so a vendor whose market is known is also searched in the language of that market (`services/vendorResearch/market.ts`). The market comes from the vendor's record only: the country stated for it (DE, AT, FR, IT, ES, NL, PT, BR; a stated US, GB, IE or AU means no market), else the country domain of its website (`.de`, `.at`, `.fr`, `.it`, `.es`, `.nl`, `.pt`, `.br`), else the legal form at the end of its name (`GmbH`, `SAS`, `S.r.l.`, `S.L.`, `B.V.`, `Lda`, `Ltda` and their kin). A `.com` or `.io` domain and a form such as `Inc.` or `S.A.` say no market, and the vendor is searched in English only. For the security incident and the legal and regulatory categories the code makes two more news searches each, with the words of the market and, for a fine, the name of its data protection authority (`Datenleck`, `CNIL sanction`, `Garante privacy sanzione`, `AEPD multa`, ...). The findings are still written in English, and a quote stays in the language of its source. The searches are recorded in the trace of the run like any other. See [web-research](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-news-in-the-vendors-language).

A vendor with no market of another language gets the authority of its country named in the legal and regulatory news instead, because the press that reports a fine or a settlement names it: `<name> FTC` and `<name> settlement` (the default, since the American authorities fine most companies that sell to American customers), `ICO fine` for a stated GB or a `.uk` domain, `DPC fine` for IE or `.ie` and `OAIC` for AU or `.au`, a stated country before the domain (`regulatorTopicsOf`, `services/vendorResearch/market.ts`). See [web-research](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-regulator-in-the-query).

## Records of public databases

For security incidents the research also looks the vendor up, with no model and no search, in public databases: the CISA catalog of known exploited vulnerabilities, the National Vulnerability Database (its critical vulnerabilities, CVSS 9.0 or more, of the last 119 days), the CVE Program's records (its critical vulnerabilities of the last 24 months), Have I Been Pwned (the breaches of a service on the vendor's domain) and the filings of the SEC (the Form 8-K reports of a material cybersecurity incident, Item 1.05, that the vendor itself filed). For availability it reads the incident history that the vendor publishes on its status page (below), and for legal and regulatory matters it looks the vendor up in the sanctions lists of the United States Treasury (the entities of the OFAC list of Specially Designated Nationals and Blocked Persons), of the United Kingdom and of the European Union, and in the US Consolidated Screening List (the export control and trade restriction lists of the Departments of Commerce, State and Treasury, without the SDN list). For AI-specific risks it looks the vendor up in lists about AI: the case studies of MITRE ATLAS, the summaries of the crash reports that NHTSA publishes under its Standing General Order, the FDA list of AI-enabled medical devices, the FDA recalls of those devices and, once its gate opens, the AI Incident Database (see [Vendor risk database](#vendor-risk-database)). The code does it before the first step of the model (`gather.ts` asks the lookup of the [vendor risk database](#vendor-risk-database), `vrdb/`, and the two lookups of `services/vendorResearch/records/` that a run still makes itself, Have I Been Pwned and the status page), and each record that matches enters the evidence as a source that was read, dated, at a public address that shows it, with the source's own words as its text: at most 5 records from the catalog, 3 from the NVD, 3 from the CVE Program, 3 from the SEC, 3 from Have I Been Pwned, 4 from the status page, 2 from each sanctions list and from the Consolidated Screening List, 3 from each of MITRE ATLAS, the NHTSA reports, the FDA recalls and the AI Incident Database and 2 from the FDA list, and a CVE that the catalog and the NVD both list is one source. The 23 lists added on 2026-11-10 (sanctions, enforcement and product safety lists, the California breach list, AI incident lists and registers, certification lists and GLEIF's LEI records; see **The third round of lists** under [Vendor risk database](#vendor-risk-database)) are looked up the same way, with 2 records from each of ten of them and 1 from each of the other thirteen.

- **A record is the vendor's only on an exact name, or on an identifier.** The database names a vendor with one string (the vendor or project of a catalog entry, the vendor part of a CPE name, the domain of a breached service, the name of a registrant of the SEC), and it must be the vendor's name or one of its other names, each as written and without the legal form, or the brand of its domain, letter for letter (the SEC, the sanctions lists and the AI lists are matched by the names alone, never by the brand of the domain, and a name that two registrants have gives nothing). A string that merely contains the name does not count. An identifier of the vendor (its LEI, its registration number, its CIK, the website that the check of the website verified) matches a list that carries it, strongly. See [Other names](#other-names) and [Vendor risk database](#vendor-risk-database).
- **A vulnerability is not a breach.** A record of the catalog, the NVD or the CVE Program is about a flaw in a product the vendor makes; it is not a sign that the vendor's own systems were breached. The analysis is told to write which it is, and that the catalog lists only vulnerabilities that are exploited in the wild while most of the NVD and the CVE Program are not. A record of the SEC is the vendor's own statement to its investors, filed with a regulator, and holds the text of Item 1.05 when the filing could be read. A record of Have I Been Pwned, whose data is licensed under CC BY 4.0 and names it as the source in every record, is an unverified source: it does not raise a label by itself.
- **The status page is found by its address and read as an Atom feed.** The status page of the vendor is guessed from its domain: `status.<domain>`, `<brand>.statuspage.io` and `<brand>status.com` (a feed found on the last two is the vendor's only when its title holds a name of the vendor). What is read is the Atom feed `/history.atom` that a Statuspage or incident.io page offers as its subscription feed, with the `robots.txt` check of the page reader. The JSON API of Statuspage is not used: the `robots.txt` of the status pages that were probed disallows `/api/` for every robot. The feed is read by a scanner written for it that takes time in proportion to the size of the feed whatever it holds (a host that the vendor does not control may serve anything), and the text of an update is the page reader's. The records are a summary (how many entries the feed holds, how many incidents the last 12 months have, how many planned maintenances) and the newest incidents of those months, three at most, with their updates in the vendor's own words; a planned maintenance is counted and is never a record. A vendor with no status page, or one that does not answer, gets nothing, and that is not a failure. A record of a status page is the vendor's own word and a statement (rule V of the [credibility](#credibility) rules), so a finding that rests on it is rated by what it is: the vendor's statement.
- **A sanctions list is a match by name unless an identifier says more.** The entries of the OFAC, UK and EU lists are copied by the scheduled job (below), and only the entities are kept: the people, the vessels and the aircraft are left out. An entity is the vendor's by name when a name it is listed under, or one of its other names, is a name of the vendor, with the legal form or without it, letter for letter; the brand of the domain is not used for a name, a name of fewer than four letters and digits is not looked up, and a name that merely contains the name of an entity is not a match. A name alone is all the match is, so the record says that it is by name only and that the entry may be another company with the same name, and the analysis is told to raise at most one risk from it, in legal and regulatory matters, rate its likelihood Possible at most, and make the action plan a check of who the vendor is against the entry. A UK or EU entry that carries the vendor's verified website, LEI or registration number (with a shared name) is a strong match and has no such note. A finding that cites only a name-only entry is labelled `low`. A UK or EU entry that the United Nations designated shows its UN reference number in its text.
- **The Consolidated Screening List is matched the same way, and keeps more kinds of entry.** The US Consolidated Screening List (`csl`) holds the export control and trade restriction lists of the Departments of Commerce (the Entity List, the Denied Persons List, the Unverified List and the Military End User List), State (the ITAR Debarred List and the Nonproliferation Sanctions) and Treasury (the sectoral, menu-based, military-industrial complex, Capta and foreign sanctions evaders lists), without the SDN list, which `ofac` holds, and the Palestinian Legislative Council list. The lists of Treasury are typed and keep their entities only, and an entity that the SDN list holds as well is left out. The lists of Commerce and State carry no entry type, so every entry is kept, people included, and a record stores a name and a country only. A Denied Persons order whose end day has passed is left out. A listing on the Entity List or the Military End User List means that an export licence is required for the entity: it is not a sanction, and the record names the list. The match is by name (weak: the label is `low`, with the note that it may be another company), and an entry of an OFAC list that carries the vendor's verified website or its LEI is strong. The lists of Commerce and State carry neither, so a vendor that really is on the Entity List is `low` with the note: the label says that the list is official and that the identity is not proven by a key, not that the finding is unreliable. The analysis is told to write such a risk as "An entity named … is on …", at most one, in legal and regulatory matters, with a likelihood of Possible at most and an action plan that checks the entity's address, country and owners and the official publication of the list. Unlike the AI lists, it needs no website: legal and regulatory matters are researched for a vendor without one, which is looked up in it by name.
- **An AI list is a match by name only.** The case studies of MITRE ATLAS, the crash report summaries of NHTSA, the list of AI-enabled medical devices of the FDA, its recalls and the incidents of the AI Incident Database carry no identifier, no website and no vendor field of a vulnerability list, so every key is a name (the name as written and without its legal form, four letters and digits or more) and every match is weak. The record says that it is by name only, the label is `low` at most whatever the class of the list (a weak record is no publisher for the Medium rule either), and the analysis is told to raise at most one risk for each of the AI Incident Database, MITRE ATLAS, NHTSA and the FDA recalls, in AI-specific risks, rate its likelihood Possible at most and make the action plan a check of who the vendor is against the entry; an entry of the FDA list raises no risk (it is used only to say, in the description of another risk, that the vendor's AI is part of a regulated medical device). The name comes from one field of each list: the developer and the deployer of an incident (split at commas; a part that names a kind of party and not a party, such as "Developers", "Scammers", "Students", "Law enforcement" or "Unknown", is dropped), the target of a case study (split into names of one to three words, generic words such as "users" or "cloud" dropped, so that a vendor called Users or Cloud matches nothing), the reporting entity of a crash report (and the text before and inside a parenthesis), and the company of a device (for a recall event, the recalling firm of each product and the company of each device on the FDA list that the recall covers). The people an incident harmed, the researchers who ran an exercise, the make of a vehicle and the operating entity are never used, so a key cannot call a victim, a researcher or a carmaker the culprit. A short brand name that is also a product (a vendor called Copilot or Cursor) can match a case study about another company's product; the note and the cap at `low` contain it. The records of these lists are about AI-specific risks, so a vendor with no website, for which that category is not researched, gets none of them.
- **A database that cannot be reached is an answer.** It is in the briefing and in the trace (one `gather` step for each database: the address asked, the outcome, the reason, the number of records, what was left out; never the text of a record), and the run goes on. The answers of Have I Been Pwned are kept in the memory of the process for 6 hours and the feed of a status page for an hour; the NVD, the SEC and GLEIF are read through the vendor risk database, which keeps their answers in its tables (6 hours, 1 hour and 24 hours). The NVD is asked at most four times in 30 seconds by all the workers together, the SEC five times a second and GLEIF 50 times a minute (windows kept in Redis, `vrdb:spacer:nvd`, `vrdb:spacer:sec` and `vrdb:spacer:gleif`, `records/sharedSpacer.ts`; a turn more than 3, 2 and 1.5 seconds off is refused, and the source is then busy, not down), and a database that failed is left alone for 5 minutes (a status page is not: a host that is not there is the usual answer to a guess, so the lookup is empty and not failed).
- **The vendor risk database is the only path for the catalog, the sanctions lists, the Consolidated Screening List, the NVD, the SEC, the CVE records and the AI lists.** The run's own lookups of the catalog, the OFAC list, the NVD and the SEC were removed when the database became always on: the records of the catalog and of the OFAC, UK and EU sanctions lists come from the copies of the [vendor risk database](#vendor-risk-database), the NVD and the SEC are read through it, the Consolidated Screening List and the AI lists (which never had a lookup of the run's own) come from its copies, and only Have I Been Pwned and the status page are still asked on the web (`sourcesBesideVrdb`, `sharedRecordsLookup` in `records/index.ts`).
- **An operator can turn it off** with `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS=false`, the status page and the vendor risk database with it (see [Configuration](#configuration)).

The details, the limits and what leaves the server are in [web-research](../infrastructure/web-research.md#records-of-public-databases).

## Vendor risk database

The vendor risk database (VRDB) keeps local copies of public lists, brought up to date by a scheduled job, and the answers of databases it reads through, and a research run looks the vendor up in them instead of on the web. It is **always on**: it has no flag of its own (`VENDOR_RESEARCH_VRDB`, which turned it on while it was tested against the golden set, was removed; the variable is read by nothing), and the one switch is `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS`, whose value `false` turns it off, with the lookups of Have I Been Pwned and the status pages (see [Configuration](#configuration)). Ten lists are copied (the CISA catalog, the OFAC, UK and EU sanctions lists, the US Consolidated Screening List, the CVE Program's records, and the four AI lists: the MITRE ATLAS case studies, the NHTSA crash reports, the FDA list of AI-enabled medical devices and the FDA recalls of those devices) and two databases are read through (the NVD and the SEC); on 2026-11-10 twenty-two more lists were added as copies and a third database, GLEIF, as a read-through (see **The third round of lists** below), which makes 32 lists copied and three databases read through; two more lists, the UN list and the AI Incident Database, are defined and tested but wait behind gates that stay closed (see **The gates** below). The design is section D4 of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-vendor-risk-database-design.md`; the code is `Servers/services/vendorResearch/vrdb/`, and the lists are declared in `vrdb/sources.ts` (`VRDB_SOURCES`).

Changes made by VerifyWise.` | One record per undertaking that a case names (a cartel case names up to 42): the number of cases, by kind (cartel, antitrust, Digital Markets Act), the first and last year of initiation and the newest 3 cases. An event (`eu_competition_cases`), dated by its newest activity. Each spelling of the undertaking (5 at most) a name key (weak). In an antitrust case that is no cartel, a name that shows no organization and occurs in one case only is taken for a person's and dropped before anything is stored (the EU rule of `connectors/kit/persons.ts`); the names of cartel and DMA cases are all kept, so a person named there, in two cases or more, or under a name that shows an organization marker stays, a residual. A company name of more than 120 characters once folded names no undertaking: it is left out of its case before anything is counted or grouped (no key, no id, no count, and neither a record nor an unreadable case), so that it is never cut into a key or an id, and the other companies of the case stay; the name that is shown is cut at 100 characters, and the id, the keys and the identifier hold the name whole. Never kept: the title of an antitrust case that is no cartel (it can carry a complainant's surname; the title of a cartel and of a DMA case is shown), the attachments, links and press releases of a case, the timelines, the sector and designation fields and the dates of publication | The Commission's page about the case data (`competition-policy.ec.europa.eu`), with `?undertaking=` and the undertaking's id |
| disclose.io program list (`disclose_io`) | `connectors/discloseIo.ts`: one JSON file, `program-list.json`, of the `disclose/diodb` repository on `raw.githubusercontent.com`, read whole: it is the list only when it has at least 1,500 rows whose program names follow one another in strictly increasing code point order, and a file of more than 20,000 rows is not read in part | Weekly, Saturday 20:08 | CC0 1.0 per the diodb LICENSE file (GitHub: CC0-1.0); the README's License section says CC BY 4.0, so the list is attributed as CC BY 4.0 requires; contact e-mails and contact URLs are not copied; decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q49). Attribution: `disclose.io diodb (https://github.com/disclose/diodb), CC0 1.0 per its LICENSE, attributed as CC BY 4.0 per its README. Entries are community contributions; adapted by VerifyWise.` | One record per organization and policy host: up to 3 programs, each with the host its policy is published on and the project's own words for its safe harbor (`full`, `partial`, `none`) and its bounty (`yes`, `no`, `partial`), else `not stated`. A state (`disclosure_program`), offered as of the copy. Each program name a name key (weak); the host of the policy a domain key (strong, against the vendor's verified domain) only when its address is used by exactly one row, the host is an apex domain, it is no shared host and no bug-bounty or document platform, and no other record carries the same host (a host that two records would carry is the key of neither, and each keeps its name keys). A program name of fewer than four letters and digits gives no name key, and an organization with no key of either kind is no record and is not counted as unreadable. Dropped before anything is stored: rows whose policy address is shared by 50 or more rows, and dead policies. A row with no program name or no policy address, or with a name of more than 80 characters or an address of more than 2,048, is counted as unreadable, and neither is ever cut. A record's id is its compact name and its host (`@none` without one); a key of more than 200 characters gives an id of its first 183 characters, a tilde and the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the whole key. Never kept: the contact e-mail addresses and contact addresses, the PGP keys, the other fields of a program, and the policy address itself (only its host) | The repository's page, `https://github.com/disclose/diodb`, with `?program=` and the organization's id |
| UK Competition and Markets Authority cases (`uk_cma`) | `connectors/ukCma.ts`: the GOV.UK Search API, the case pages of the CMA, in pages of 1,000 (3 pages on 2026-11-10, at most 5), through `connectors/govukSearch.ts` | Weekly, Tuesday 08:16 | Open Government Licence v3.0 (https://www.nationalarchives.gov.uk/doc/open-government-licence/version/3/): the GOV.UK search metadata of the CMA case pages (title, case type, state, outcome, dates); case documents, party submissions and personal data are excluded (Q52). A case is kept when it can carry an intervention or an enforcement outcome (a merger that is open or ended in a phase 2 prohibition, a phase 2 clearance with remedies or phase 1 undertakings in lieu; a case of another type unless it closed without action); every other case is read and left out, and so is a kept case that names no party. A kept case whose title is longer than 500 characters, or empty once folded, is unreadable and counted as such. A party that begins with Mr, Mrs, Ms or Miss (with or without a full stop, and a blank after it) is dropped, and a former or new name of that form is left out of the names of its party; the case stays for its other parties, and its title is quoted among their newest cases as the CMA gives it. A party with a name of more than 120 characters, or of fewer than four letters and digits once its legal form is off, is dropped. Entries adapted by VerifyWise.` | One record per manufacturer that an investigation names (an investigation counts once for each manufacturer it names): the number of investigations, the first and last year of opening, the counts of the latest 4 years of opening (counted back from the newest day in the file, never from the clock), how many are linked to a recall campaign, how many of those opened in the last 7 calendar years have no close date recorded, how many name a driver assistance or automated driving component and the newest 3 investigations (id, opening and closing day, component and subject). An event (`defect_investigations`), dated by its newest activity. The manufacturer's spellings (5 at most, and the text before and inside a parenthesis) are name keys (weak); the make of a vehicle never is. A manufacturer has a record only when some name of it, as written or without its legal form, has four letters and digits or more; its id is its compact name without the legal form, and a manufacturer whose id is shorter than four characters is keyed by its full name. A manufacturer's name that does not fit in 80 characters once cleaned is never cut into an id or a key: its line is unreadable and counted, as is a line that does not have 11 fields, whose investigation id or days are not in their form, or whose chosen fields are not UTF-8; a line that names no manufacturer (an empty cell, a placeholder) is no entity and is counted apart. The file is the list only when it holds at least 4,000 investigations and 400 manufacturers with a record, and no more than one line in twenty has the wrong shape; no person filter is applied, since every name of the real file was read when the list was designed and none is a person. Never kept: the summary text (it names petitioners and complainants), the make, model and year, and the campaign number (kept only as a yes or no) | NHTSA's page of its datasets and APIs, with `?dataset=investigations&manufacturer=` and the manufacturer's id |
| FedRAMP Marketplace (`fedramp`) | `connectors/fedramp.ts`: `data.json` of the `FedRAMP/marketplace-fedramp-gov-data` repository on `raw.githubusercontent.com`, read whole: the file's own counts are checked on every sync (`Metrics.total`, `ready` and `in_process` against the products, and the ids of `AtoMapping` against the authorized products), it must hold at least 400 products and its earliest authorization must be on or before 2014-12-31, any other file is not the list, and a file of more than 5,000 products or mappings fails as too large | 19:06 daily | None published; a work of the US General Services Administration (17 U.S.C. §105) in GSA's FedRAMP/marketplace-fedramp-gov-data repository: only GSA's status fields are copied (provider, offering, status and its date, impact level, authorisation type, agency count, UEI); the host of each offering's website is used as a match key only and never shown; logos, provider descriptions and contact data are not copied; decided on delegation on 2026-11-10 (Q48). Attribution: `Source: US General Services Administration, FedRAMP Marketplace data. Not endorsed by GSA or FedRAMP. Entries adapted by VerifyWise.` | One record per provider: its cloud offerings by status (FedRAMP Authorized, FedRAMP Ready, FedRAMP In Process, Agency In Process) and the 3 shown first (name, id, status and the day it began, impact level, authorization type, number of agencies that use it). A state (`cloud_authorization_status`), offered as of the copy; a provider that leaves the file is `delisted`. Each spelling of the provider (5 at most) a name key (weak); the host of a product's website (the first of the field, without `www.`) a domain key (strong, against the vendor's verified domain) only when it is an apex domain that is no shared host and is claimed by exactly one provider of the whole file, and its first label has three characters or more and is the provider's compact short name, contains it (a name of four characters or more) or is the beginning of it (a label of four characters or more); a short name inside another company's longer label, a label that is only a word inside the name, a subdomain and a host that two providers claim give no key; a UEI (12 letters and digits) a `regno:` key (strong only beside the name). At most 5 hosts, 3 UEIs and 10 package ids are kept, and a provider with no name key and no host that qualifies is no record. A product with no usable id (3 to 20 letters and digits), offering, status or day of its status, or whose provider is longer than 100 characters, cannot be read and is counted, and a website field of more than 300 characters has no host. The record says that a listing is an authorization status and not a security assurance. Never kept: the logo, the provider's descriptions, the contact data, the assessors and the website itself; the service and deployment models and the initial authorizing agency are not carried either, since no sentence of a record shows them | The repository's page, `https://github.com/FedRAMP/marketplace-fedramp-gov-data`, with `?provider=` and the provider's id |
| Epoch AI Data on AI Models (`epoch_ai`) | `connectors/epochAi.ts`: one CSV file, `all_ai_models.csv`, on `epoch.ai` (the companies file is not fetched), a conditional request with the file's weak ETag; the file is the list only when its header holds the columns that are read, `Model` is one text per row and never twice, it has at least 3,000 rows and at least nine in ten of the rows that have an organization line up, and a file of more than 20,000 rows fails as too large | Weekly, Thursday 23:39 | Creative Commons Attribution (Epoch AI's data pages: 'free to use, distribute, and reproduce provided the source and authors are credited'; version 4.0 on the AI companies page, unstated on the AI models documentation): the AI models dataset; accepted on 2026-11-10 (Q43). Attribution: `Epoch AI, 'Data on AI models', published online at epoch.ai (https://epoch.ai/data/ai-models-documentation), under the Creative Commons Attribution licence. Counted and adapted by VerifyWise; not endorsed by Epoch AI.` | One record per organization that the dataset labels `Industry` (a model counts once for each organization it names): the number of models, the first publication year and the newest day, how many are frontier models and how many have open weights, and the newest 3 models. A state (`ai_model_developer`), offered as of the copy. Each organization a name key (weak); an item is an organization of the Industry kind only when the two comma-joined lists (organizations and their labels) have the same number of items and the label at its position is exactly `Industry`, so a row whose lists differ gives no key, and neither does a list cell of more than 2,000 characters, of which nothing is read; an item with fewer than four letters and digits gives no key. A row with no model name, with an organization item of more than 200 characters, or with a flag cell of more than a megabyte is unreadable and counted. The title and the text show the first spelling cut at 80 characters, and the identifiers and the names hold it whole. Never kept: the authors (natural persons), the Hugging Face developer id, the abstracts, links and notes, and the technical columns | The page that documents the data on `epoch.ai`, with `?organisation=` and the organization's id |
| C2PA Conforming Products List (`c2pa_conforming`) | `connectors/c2paConforming.ts`: `conforming-products-list.json` of the `c2pa-org/conformance-public` repository on `raw.githubusercontent.com` | 16:49 daily | CC BY 4.0 (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/): the c2pa-org/conformance-public repository's LICENSE; the C2PA name and marks are not licensed and are used only to name the source. Attribution: `C2PA Conforming Products List, c2pa-org/conformance-public repository, CC BY 4.0 (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). Adapted by VerifyWise; not endorsed by the C2PA.` | One record per applicant with at least one conformant product: the number of conformant products (and how many are generators and validators), the day of the newest conformance, up to 5 product names and the number of listed products with another status; an applicant with none conformant has no record. A state (`c2pa_conformance`), offered as of the copy. The applicant, and when it differs the certificate organization of a product, a name key (weak). A product whose applicant states that it is the business of a person is dropped as soon as its applicant is read, whatever else is wrong with it, and counts nowhere, not even as unreadable; the terms are, in any case, sole proprietor, sole trader, `Einzelunternehmen`, `Einzelkaufmann`, freelance, self-employed, `e.K.` and `e.U.`, and the like of other countries (`Einzelfirma`, `eenmanszaak`, `entreprise individuelle`, `EIRL`, `auto-entrepreneur`, `micro-entrepreneur`, `empresario individual`, `autonomo`, `ditta individuale`, `enskild firma`, `enkeltpersonforetak`), and `Sole Prop.` and `Inh.`; an organization name that says so is no key either. An applicant written `<name>, dba <business>` (also `d/b/a`, `doing business as`, `t/a` and `trading as`) is the business when the part before the mark holds a letter or a digit and no legal form and no organization word, and that part is then cut out of everything the record holds. A product is unreadable when its record id is no UUID or occurs twice (both are left out), its applicant is empty, has no letter or digit or is longer than 100 characters, its status is no text, or its conformance is no day that exists. The file is the list only when it is an array with at least 150 conformant products and at least 100 applicants that have one, and a file of more than 10,000 products fails as too large. Never kept: the products' own web addresses (never fetched, no key) and the technical fields | The list's file in the repository, `https://github.com/c2pa-org/conformance-public/blob/main/conforming-products/conforming-products-list.json`, with `?applicant=` and the applicant's id |
| GLEIF LEI records (`gleif`) | `connectors/gleif.ts`, read through: one request to `api.gleif.org` (`lei-records?filter[lei]=<LEI>`) for a vendor with a stored LEI, and for nothing else: never a search by name | Asked at run time; an answer is kept 24 hours | CC0 1.0 (https://creativecommons.org/publicdomain/zero/1.0/) under GLEIF's LEI Data Terms of Use (https://www.gleif.org/en/meta/lei-data-terms-of-use): access only through GLEIF's access services, no claim of rights in LEI data, and no statement that implies that VerifyWise or its products are provided, supported, authorised or associated by or with GLEIF (liquidated damages of CHF 100,000 per case of non-compliance); accepted on 2026-11-10 (Q53). Attribution: `GLEIF LEI data (CC0 1.0), from the Global Legal Entity Identifier Foundation's API. VerifyWise is not associated with GLEIF, and GLEIF does not provide, support, authorise or endorse VerifyWise or its products.` | One record for the LEI: the legal name, jurisdiction, legal form code and category, the entity status, the registration status, the UTC days of the next renewal and the last update, whether a direct and an ultimate parent are reported (a parent is not looked up) and the golden copy that the answer is as of. The record says that GLEIF is not associated with VerifyWise, and that a lapsed registration means the data were not renewed on time. A state (`lei_record`), dated by the last update. The one key is the `lei:` of the vendor's identifier (strong); a name never matches. Never kept: addresses, the BIC, MIC, OCID, QCC, GEM and S&P numbers, the issuer and how it validated the entity, the number in the entity's own register, other names, successor and associated entities, and links | The LEI's record at GLEIF, `https://api.gleif.org/api/v1/lei-records/<LEI>` |
