- **The tables.** Four shared tables without `organization_id`, which hold no tenant data and are written only by the sync and the lookup (`vrdb_*` in the `sharedTables` of `scripts/auditTenantIsolationCoverage.ts`): `vrdb_sources`, one row per list, upserted from `vrdb/sources.ts` at every sync, with the state of its copy (the last attempt and success, a fixed error code and never a message, the validators of the last download, the number of active records; staleness is computed from `last_success_at`, never stored); `vrdb_records`, one row per record of a list with its typed match keys (`name:…`, `kev:…`, `domain:…`) under a GIN index, where a record that left its list keeps its row with another status; `vrdb_sync_runs`, one row per sync attempt with the hash of the bodies it read, kept 90 days (`SYNC_RUNS_KEPT_DAYS`); and `vrdb_lookups`, the state of each key of a read-through source (the key, when it was last answered, `fetched_at`, null for a key whose first answer failed; when it may be asked again, `expires_at`; the outcome and the number of records). A partial index on the ids of rejected records serves the lookup's filter of rejected CVEs (`20261031031802-vrdb-read-through.js`). The raw files are hashed, never kept. One tenant-scoped table, `vendor_vrdb_matches`, holds the records that a vendor of an organization matched, with the method and the strength of the match; it is in the tenant-isolation registry (`vendor-vrdb-matches.isolation.test.ts`). The queries are in `utils/vrdb.utils.ts`; the migration is `20261030223825-vendor-risk-database.js`.
- **The sync** (`syncSource` in `vrdb/sync.ts`). Each list has a BullMQ scheduler of its own on the queue `vendor-risk-database` (`vrdb/queue.ts`), at the minute of the table above. The backend registers the schedulers at start (`jobs/producer.ts`) while `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS` is on, and removes them otherwise, so turning it off stops every download. At the same start it queues a first sync of every active list that is copied whole and has no copy yet (`last_success_at` is null, or the list has no row), one job id per list (`vrdb_sync_first_<id>`), so that a new deployment does not leave the runs without a list until its scheduler's minute (up to six hours for a list copied every six hours, a day for a daily list and a week for a weekly one); when the states cannot be read it logs a fixed line and queues none. It queues none for the CVE records: a list read by its changes gets its first copy from the baseline that an operator loads (below), and a first sync before that would only record a failed sync at every start. The worker of `vrdb/worker.ts` runs in the process of the other workers (`jobs/worker.ts`), takes one sync at a time, checks the switch and the list's gate again, and BullMQ tries a sync that failed on the way three times with an exponential back-off from one minute (`VRDB_SYNC_JOB_OPTIONS`); a sync the list refused (the file was not the list, did not parse, was empty, too large or shrank, or the feed named no file) or whose answer asked for a wait (`Retry-After`) is not tried again, since a download a minute later would get the same file or come too soon, and the list's next scheduled sync asks again. Two known limits follow. A real loss of more than a fifth of a list's active records is refused as `shrank` at every scheduled sync while the list stays that small, so the copy keeps its old records and goes stale until an operator steps in; on the smallest lists that is a loss of 4 records (`uk_ofsi`, 19) or 3 (`fmti`, 11). A weekly list whose sync fails after its three attempts, is refused or is told to wait is not asked again until its next weekly schedule, so its copy shows as out of date (stale after 192 hours) from the eighth day after its last good sync until the next one. A sync downloads the list through the page guard's conditional GET (`guardedConditionalFetch`, see [web-research](../infrastructure/web-research.md#records-of-public-databases)): a 304, or bodies that hash as the last success's did, is `unchanged`. Otherwise the list is parsed (the XML lists by the scanner of `vrdb/xmlScan.ts`) and written in one transaction (`applyVrdbSnapshotQuery`): a new record is inserted active, a changed one takes its new content, one that had left the list is active again, and an active record that the list no longer holds becomes `withdrawn` (the catalog, the AI lists and every copy added on 2026-11-10 but two) or `delisted` (a sanctions list, the Consolidated Screening List, Canada's sanctions list and the FedRAMP Marketplace; `missingStatus` in `vrdb/sources.ts`). The hash includes the connector's `version`, but the sync compares hashes only of what it downloads: after a change of how a connector reads its list (a new `version`), a list that answers 304 keeps its copy until its source changes (the lists that the kit's factory builds do not: the first download after a new `version` sends no validators, see **The list kit** under **The third round of lists**). To have it read again at once, set `etag` and `last_modified` of its row in `vrdb_sources` to NULL; the next sync downloads and parses the list. The recalls' composite value is kept in `last_modified` and is cleared the same way.
- **A download that is not the whole list never reaches the copy.** A body cut at its limit (`too_large`), a document that is not the list (`wrong_root`), one that parsed with errors (`parse_errors`: an XML file whose root never closes, a cut short download that a server framed well; an OFAC row of the wrong number of fields, 12 in `SDN.CSV` and 5 in `ALT.CSV`, a file that ends inside a quoted field, one of more rows than the list can hold, and an `ALT.CSV` without a row), an empty one (`empty`) and one with more than a fifth fewer records than the copy's active ones (`shrank`, `SHRINK_LIMIT`) fail the sync and leave the copy as it was. A failure stores and logs a fixed code and the list's id, never a message: an error can carry an address, and the address of the EU file carries a token (Q2), which the EU connector reads from the operator's RSS feed at every sync and keeps in a local variable only (`feed_without_file` when the feed names no such file).
- **The match** (`vrdb/keys.ts`), with no model. The vendor's keys are each of its names and other names, compacted as written and without the legal form, and the brand of its domain, as a KEV key and as the vendor of a CVE record's affected product (`cve:`); the vendor part of its CPE names (`cpe:`); each of its names and other names (never the brand) of four characters or more as a name key, since a sanctions list and an AI list are matched by names; each domain that the website check verified as the vendor's (`verifiedDomainsOf` in `preflight.ts`: the recorded domain when its home page was read and names the vendor, with the domain it moved to when that is the vendor's too); and the keys of its [identifiers](#create-vendor): its LEI (`lei:`), its registration number (`regno:`, six letters and digits or more) and its CIK (`cik:`). A record's keys are the names, KEV vendor, CPE or CVE vendor, website hosts and numbers that its list gives (a number that passes the LEI check is an `lei:` key, any other with a digit a `regno:` key, `identifierKeysOf`), and a record is the vendor's when the two share a key, the surest first (`matchOf`): an LEI, a verified domain, a stored CIK, a CPE, KEV or CVE vendor names the vendor the way the list does and is a strong match; a registration number is strong only when the record also shares a name with the vendor, since a number is unique only in its register; a CIK found by the vendor's name (Q3) and a name alone are weak (this may be another company). A host where many have pages (`sharedHosts.ts`: the platforms of the registry where users publish, and code hosts, site builders, app hosts, `t.me`, `vk.com` and `linktr.ee`, with the hosts under them) is never a key, on either side, and its brand is never the vendor's (`records/match.ts`): a LinkedIn page or `acme.github.io` says nothing about who it is.
- **The lookup** (`createVrdbLookup`, `offerRecords` and `sharedVrdbLookup` in `vrdb/lookup.ts`). `gatherSources` (`gather.ts`) asks it beside the web lookups of the other databases (Have I Been Pwned and the status page). The keys are matched against the copies in two queries; only active records are offered, the strong matches first, then the newest; a catalog entry older than the lookback of its category is left out and counted as old (a designation, a listing of the Consolidated Screening List (`restricted_party`) and an authorization of the FDA list are states, so the lookback does not apply to them; an AI incident, an ATLAS case study, a summary of crash reports and a recall of an AI-enabled device (`ai_device_recall`) are events, and it does apply to them, by their own day: `isEvent` in `vrdb/lookup.ts`; of the lists of 2026-11-10, the 12 that record matters are events, dated by their newest matter, and the others, Canada's sanctions entries and the registers and status lists, are states: see **The third round of lists** below); at most 5 records of the catalog, 3 of the NVD, of the SEC, of the CVE records, of ATLAS, of NHTSA, of the FDA recalls and of the AI Incident Database, and 2 of each sanctions list, of the Consolidated Screening List and of the FDA list are offered (`MAX_RECORDS`; of the lists of 2026-11-10, 2 of ten and 1 of the other thirteen), the rest counted as more; a CVE that one list offered is not offered again by a later one (the catalog, then the NVD, then the CVE records), and a vulnerability whose CVE any list rejected is never offered. A record's text is the list's own, the lookup note for a match by name alone, and the day of the copy ("As of …"). A list with no copy yet is `failed`, and the screen names it under "Not checked"; a copy that has not been brought up to date for the hours of its list (`staleAfterHours` in `vrdb/sources.ts`: 48, and 192 for a list copied once a week) is stale, and its records are offered all the same. `gatherSources` gives the lookup the verified domains of the website check (`verifiedDomains`). The matches of the offered records are kept in `vendor_vrdb_matches`. When the database cannot be read the run goes on without the records, and every list is `failed`. With `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS=false` the lookup reads nothing and every list is `off`. A list behind a closed gate is neither looked up nor in the coverage.
- **The read-through** (`vrdb/readThrough.ts`, spec D4.8 step 3). A read-through source is asked at run time by a key of the vendor (`keyOf` of its connector: the NVD by the CPE vendor token, the SEC by the stored CIK, else by the registrant whose name is the vendor's, GLEIF by the stored LEI and by nothing else, so that a vendor with no stored LEI has no key and the source is `not_researched` for it; every SEC request names `VerifyWise tech@verifywise.ai` in its User-Agent), and its answer is stored in the tables of the copies: the records in `vrdb_records`, the state of the key in `vrdb_lookups`. An answer younger than the source's hours (6 for the NVD, 1 for the SEC, 24 for GLEIF) is not asked again, by any run of any organization. An expired or new key is asked within the run's 8 seconds (`READ_THROUGH_TIMEOUT_MS`), on a turn of the source's spacer, which all the workers share in Redis (`records/sharedSpacer.ts`: the NVD 4 requests in 30 seconds, waiting 3 seconds at most; the SEC 5 a second, waiting 2 seconds at most; GLEIF 50 requests in 60 seconds, waiting 1.5 seconds at most); the records of the answer are inserted or brought up to date in one transaction with the key's state (`saveReadThroughAnswerQuery`). An answer that holds its whole window (the NVD's: its `totalResults` fits the page of 50; the connector's `coversSince`) takes the key off a stored record of the key published in that window that it lacks: the CVE was rejected, re-scored below critical or moved off the vendor, so it is no longer offered for the key; the record keeps its status and its other keys, and a pending finding that cites it keeps its label (no status changed, so the status job does not run). A record published before the window aged out of it and stays, offered as of the last answer that held it (`retrieved_at`). A CVE an answer marks Rejected is stored rejected; since the query asks for critical CVEs, a rejected CVE without a critical score simply leaves the answer. The SEC's answers cover no window: a filing is never taken back. A failure keeps the stored records in use, makes the source `failed` in the coverage, and leaves the key alone for 5 minutes (`RETRY_AFTER_FAILURE_MS`), unless the run stopped or the turn was too far off, which says the source is busy, not down. The coverage of a read-through source is that of a list, dated by its answer, and the screen lists it as one.
- **The gates** (`vrdb/gates.ts`). A list that waits for a person is defined, tested and documented, but while its gate is closed it is never downloaded, scheduled or looked up, no run shows it, and no script loads it. A gate opens by a change of that file, once the question it waits for is decided. **Q4**, the SEC: decided on 2026-10-31, and the gate is open. The User-Agent that the SEC asks of automated access names the e-mail address of whoever runs the client, so that the SEC can write before it limits or blocks the client. The address is `tech@verifywise.ai`, the Technical Support contact that verifywise.ai/contact publishes (`SEC_CONTACT`; a role and not a person, and a constant and not a setting, so that every deployment declares the same identity), and every SEC request, the read-through, the text of a filing and the header of a filing alike, carries `VerifyWise tech@verifywise.ai`. **Q6**, the UN list: the written permission of the United Nations (`UN_PERMISSION_GRANTED`). Its terms of use, read on 2026-10-31, allow personal, non-commercial use only, so the gate stays closed; the element names of `connectors/un.ts` were checked against the real file the same day (274 entities, no error). The UN's designations reach a run through the UK and EU lists all the same: on 2026-10-30 the UK list held every entity of the UN list, 269 of the 274 with their UN reference number, which a UK or EU record shows. **Q7**, the CVE records: decided on 2026-10-31, and the gate is open. The CVE Terms of Use for this use were accepted by the product owner on 2026-10-31, without a separate legal review, and the product owner permitted the download of the baseline file (`CVE_TERMS_APPROVED`). Q5, the OFAC list, was accepted by the product owner on 2026-10-31 as well (it is not a gate: the list publishes no terms, as a work of the US Government). **Q29**, the AI Incident Database: decided on 2026-11-05 to build the list behind a closed gate (`AIID_PERMISSION_GRANTED`, false). The collections of its weekly export that the database reads (incidents, entities and classifications) are licensed CC BY-SA 4.0, but the site's general terms bar anyone from selling or commercially exploiting any aspect of the site without express permission, so the licence and the terms disagree on their face, and the operator, the Responsible AI Collaborative, names a contact for such questions (info@incidentdatabase.ai). The gate opens on the operator's written confirmation that a commercial product may store the export and show records derived from it under CC BY-SA 4.0, or on the product owner's explicit written acceptance of that risk. **To open it,** an operator sets `AIID_PERMISSION_GRANTED` to `true` in `vrdb/gates.ts` and deploys: at its next start the backend registers the daily scheduler of the list and queues its first sync, and from then on the list is in the coverage of the runs (under "Not checked" until the first sync has succeeded). Before it opens, a reviewer re-reads 20 random records for the names of private individuals, because the editors' descriptions name victims and the accused. The same review looks at the developer and deployer lines and at the match keys: the connector drops kinds of party but not names of people, and the export of 2026-11-02 names some public figures there; the review decides whether such a name may stay or a person-name filter is built first. **Q31**, the NHTSA crash reports, and **Q32**, MITRE ATLAS, were accepted on 2026-11-05 under the product owner's delegation and are not gates: NHTSA publishes no licence and its files are a work of the US Government (17 U.S.C. §105), accepted in the way of Q5, and the LICENSE file of the ATLAS data repository, the Apache License 2.0, governs over the README's release marking; the repository has no NOTICE file (checked on 2026-11-05), so section 4(d) of the licence adds nothing to the attribution. **Q33**, the US Consolidated Screening List, and **Q34**, the FDA recalls, were accepted on 2026-11-07 under the product owner's delegation and are not gates. The Consolidated Screening List publishes no licence: it is a compilation by the International Trade Administration (ITA) of lists that the Departments of Commerce, State and Treasury publish, a work of the US Government (17 U.S.C. §105), accepted in the way of Q5 and Q31 without a separate legal review; ITA's developer terms allow a service to search, display and analyze it and ask for a notice, which the attribution gives in an adapted form because the database reads the downloadable file and not the Data API that the notice names; the terms bar modifying the content while naming ITA as its source, so every record says that it is adapted. openFDA's device recall data is CC0 1.0 (credit requested, not required), and the GMDN content of its `openfda` block, licensed separately, is never read or stored. **Q35 to Q53**, the lists of 2026-11-10 (see **The third round of lists** below), were decided on 2026-11-10 under the product owner's delegation, and none is a gate: each list is built open. **Q35** (the NHTSA defect investigations) and **Q36** (the NHTSA recalls) were accepted in the way of Q31, as works of the US Government that NHTSA publishes with no licence: the investigation summaries, which name petitioners, the manufacturer-supplied fields 25 to 27 of the recalls file and its Part 573 texts are never stored. **Q37**, the FDA device enforcement reports (CC0 1.0), is a fold into the FDA recalls (Q34) and no list of its own: only the class and the classification day of a recalled product are carried, and the licence and attribution of the `fda_device_recalls` row name both endpoints. **Q38**, the PipeRoll registry (CC BY 4.0; the MIT licence of its repository covers the tooling), never reads its `aiid_incident_id` field, so that nothing of the AI Incident Database (Q29) passes through it. **Q39**, the AI Hallucination Cases database (CC BY 4.0), carries a Content-Signal `ai-train=no` in its operator's `robots.txt`: its data never trains or fine-tunes a model, and a record holds counts per tool and no party, court or case text. **Q40**, the Government of Canada AI Register, takes the licence of its dataset record on `open.canada.ca` (OGL-Canada 2.0), not the terms of the Canada.ca pages. **Q41**, the US federal AI use case inventory, is a work of the US Government accepted in the way of Q31 and Q33. **Q42**, the Dutch Algorithm Register, names no licence: its copyright page permits reuse of its content, which is a new precedent. **Q43** (Epoch AI's data on AI models, a Creative Commons Attribution licence), **Q44** (the Foundation Model Transparency Index, CC BY 4.0, shown as a count of the published file's own cells and never as the index's score), **Q45** (DG Competition's case data under the Commission reuse notice: the antitrust, cartel and Digital Markets Act files only, with no attachment or press release and no person's name), **Q46** (Canada's sanctions list: the OGL-Canada licence of the dataset record is taken as the specification that the Canada.ca terms reserve, and only entities are copied), **Q47** (Colombia's SIC sanctions, CC BY-SA 4.0, so the adapted records are shared under it), **Q48** (the FedRAMP Marketplace, a work of the US General Services Administration: only its status fields), **Q49** (the disclose.io program list, CC0 1.0 per its LICENSE and attributed as CC BY 4.0 per its README), **Q50** (the California Attorney General's breach list, in the public domain by its conditions page, without the sample notices), **Q51** (the UK algorithmic transparency records, of which 13 of 155 come from bodies that are not Crown bodies and rely on GOV.UK's statement that its content is under the OGL v3.0), **Q52** (the CMA cases, the OFSI notices and the MHRA lists, OGL v3.0) and **Q53** (GLEIF, CC0 1.0 under terms that bar implying an association with GLEIF) were accepted on conditions that the licence and attribution strings in the table above record. **Q39 and Q42 are built open on delegation, for the product owner's confirmation:** if the owner reverses either, the list gets a gate, as the AI Incident Database did (Q29), and its copy is deleted.
- **The AI lists** (Q30 to Q32, 2026-11-05; spec sections D2.14 to D2.18 and D4.12). Four lists about AI enter the database as copies, in the category AI-specific risks (`ai_specific`): the MITRE ATLAS case studies (`atlas`), NHTSA's crash reports (`nhtsa_sgo`), the FDA list of AI-enabled medical devices (`fda_ai_devices`) and the AI Incident Database (`aiid`, behind the gate Q29). Each copy is stale after 48 hours, and a record that its list no longer holds becomes `withdrawn`. A fifth list, the FDA recalls of the devices on the FDA list (`fda_device_recalls`), was added on 2026-11-07 (below).
  - **Record types, class and strength.** `ai_incident` (AIID), `ai_case_study` (ATLAS) and `crash_report_summary` (NHTSA) are events: `isEvent` in `vrdb/lookup.ts` holds them with `vulnerability` and `sec_filing`, so the 24-month lookback applies to them by their own day (the incident's day, the start date of a case study, the first day of the newest incident month of the crash reports) and the record is offered with it. `ai_device_authorizations` (FDA) is a state, like a sanctions entry: no lookback, and the record is offered as of the copy. `aiid` is classed unverified (an aggregator of allegations), `atlas` trusted press (MITRE curates the case studies with references), `nhtsa_sgo` and `fda_ai_devices` official; all four are weak, so a record gives `low` support and the name-only note whatever its class.
  - **Granularity and privacy.** NHTSA is summarised per reporting entity and file and not per report (one entity has thousands), and the VIN, location, time, narrative, operating entity and every column of confidential business information are never read into a record. The AIID record leaves out the people harmed, the editors' ids and notes and every report text (the licence excludes them too), and the ATLAS record leaves out references, relationships and procedure steps. The address of an NHTSA or FDA record carries a slug of a company name in a query parameter, never a person, so that no two records share an address.
  - **Hostile text.** The description of an ATLAS case study can carry a prompt-injection payload (some do in the real file). The connector first brings the text to the form it finally reads in: it folds compatibility forms (a full-width `<` is a `<`), removes control characters and characters a reader cannot see, and decodes entities until nothing changes, so that a tag written as entities is a tag (stricter than decoding last, which would keep the text of a block written that way). It removes each `<atlas-example>` block whole (an unclosed one to the end of the text) and starts the pass again when that changed the text, then strips every other tag and keeps the text between them, reduces links to their text and drops `[[reference]]`. It repeats the pass until the text no longer changes, then keeps the first paragraph, cut at a word boundary: 600 characters for the summary, 200 for the name, 300 for the target and for the actor. A text that never settles becomes empty. An AIID description loses its tags and links, is decoded and cut in the same order, and gets the same 600-character cap, with `[shortened]` after a cut one. The YAML of ATLAS is read under the connector's own guards: it is refused when it has an alias, an explicit tag, a repeated key, a second document or more than a million lexemes (the package's check for repeated keys is quadratic, so the connector makes it in linear time). The record then goes through the same escaping and untrusted-data line as every source.
  - **Downloads and limits.** Each list is downloaded only from its own host, through the page guard: `incidentdatabase.ai` (the snapshots page, 2 MiB, 30 s) and the host of its public export bucket (the file, 16 MiB, 120 s, its final address after redirects on that host for a 304 as well as a 200; the zip inside it at most 64 entries, 48 MiB for an entry and 64 MiB in all; the sheet at most 20,000 rows and 256 columns); `raw.githubusercontent.com/mitre-atlas/atlas-data/` (the manifest 256 KiB and the YAML 8 MiB, each of the repository's own path after redirects, a 304 of the YAML included); `static.nhtsa.gov/odi/ffdd/sgo-2021-01/` (three files of 32 MiB and 60,000 reports at most each, about 5.3 MB in all, downloaded whole every day because a snapshot holds one pair of validators, and the hash finds an unchanged copy); and `www.fda.gov` (2 MiB; the answer must come from that host and the header row must hold exactly the six columns). The other lists use a conditional request. An address never enters a record, a log line or an error text. A row that cannot be read (no id, no title or name, a date that does not parse) is skipped and counted, the sync logs how many it left out (a warning with the list's id and a number, `vendor risk database: <id> left out N records that do not parse`, never a row) and it fails as `parse_errors` only when they exceed max(10, 1% of the rows); a file that is not the list (a missing column, an unknown format version, no sheet `Incidents`, a YAML that breaks a guard) is `wrong_root`, and a snapshots page that links no export is `feed_without_file`.
  - **Attribution and licence.** The row of each list in `vrdb_sources` carries the licence and the attribution of the table above, and the Data sources line of the coverage shows the attribution of each list the run asked. MITRE's copyright year is the current UTC year, read at each use. The attribution of the AI Incident Database says that the records are adapted by VerifyWise and shared under CC BY-SA 4.0: share-alike applies to the stored and shown record texts, and leaving out the attribution or the share-alike note is a licence breach even where the use itself is allowed.
  - **How to read the coverage row of a list that has no copy yet.** A run's coverage has one row for each active list. `found` and `none` mean that the list had a copy and the run asked it: the screen shows it under "Checked" with its count, and a list with `none` also under "No records in … as of …" with the day of its copy (the 22 lists of 2026-11-10 but Canada's sanctions list are named under "Checked" only when they offered a record, and are counted in its last item when they held none; see **The third round of lists** below). `failed` with no day of copy means that the list has no copy yet: a new deployment, or one just upgraded, has none of the lists that the upgrade added (the three open AI lists of 2026-11-05, then the Consolidated Screening List and the FDA recalls of 2026-11-07, then the 22 copies of 2026-11-10) until their first sync, which the backend queues at its start (a list whose syncs have all failed so far shows the same row), so a run in that window shows them under "Not checked" and its findings rest on none of them. That is a gap and not a clean result, and it ends when the first sync succeeds. A copy older than the hours of its list (48, or 192 for a list copied once a week) is stale: the run asks it all the same, and the screen says "not up to date". `not_researched` (the vendor has no website, so AI-specific risks, the FDA recalls among them, and certifications were not researched; a read-through source that the vendor has no key for, GLEIF without a stored LEI, is `not_researched` as well; the Consolidated Screening List belongs to legal and regulatory matters, which are researched with no website) and `off` (the records are turned off) put the list on no "Checked" or "Not checked" line. The AI Incident Database has no row at all while its gate is closed, so its absence from "Checked" and "Not checked" does not say that no incident names the vendor.
- **The US Consolidated Screening List** (Q33, 2026-11-07; spec sections D2.19 and D4.13). `csl` copies the file that the International Trade Administration publishes at `data.trade.gov` (about 34 MB of JSON): the export control and trade restriction lists of Commerce, State and Treasury, without the SDN list, which `ofac` holds. No gate is involved. The category is legal and regulatory matters, the record type `restricted_party`, a state: a listing in force does not age out, so the lookback does not apply and the record is offered as of the copy. The class is official, and a match by name is weak.
  - **Kept and dropped.** A list is the code in the parentheses of an entry's `source`. Kept: the Entity List, the Denied Persons List, the Unverified List and the Military End User List (Commerce), the ITAR Debarred List and the Nonproliferation Sanctions (State), and the Sectoral Sanctions Identifications, Non-SDN Menu-Based Sanctions, Non-SDN Chinese Military-Industrial Complex Companies, Capta and Foreign Sanctions Evaders lists (Treasury). Dropped: the SDN list (`ofac` holds it) and the Palestinian Legislative Council list (78 individuals, no company). An entry of a list that is not in the map is skipped and counted and is no error, so a new list does not fail the sync, and a renamed list that was large fails through the shrink check. The lists of Treasury are typed and keep the entries of type `Entity`; the lists of Commerce and State are not typed and keep every entry. A Denied Persons entry whose `end_date` is before the day of the sync (UTC) is dropped. The projection of `download` applies that (with a clock that a test sets) and `parse` never does, because the sync hashes the bodies and answers `unchanged` when the hash is that of the last success, so a filter that depends on the day must change the bodies. While ITA's file keeps its `ETag`, which ITA regenerates daily, the answer is a 304 and nothing is projected, so an expired order stays in the copy until the next regeneration. An entry whose `entity_number` is that of an SDN entry of the same file is dropped (90 entries of the Sectoral list on 2026-11-07), since `ofac` is the authority for it. The file of 2026-11-07 gave 5,172 records.
  - **Granularity and keys.** One record per list and name (the name compared as letters and digits), so that a name with several orders is one record whose text says how many entries of that name the list holds, and the record does not depend on the file's own ids, which are hashes for the lists other than OFAC's. Each name, other name (100 read at most for an entry) and `Issuer Name` id is a name key (as written and without the legal form, 4 characters or more, 200 at most for a record); the host of each `Website` id is a domain key (strong, as for the UK and EU lists); the ids of the types Legal Entity Number, Registration Number, Registration ID, Company Number, Business Registration Number, Public Registration Number, Unified Social Credit Code (USCC) and Tax ID No. give `lei:` or `regno:` keys (an LEI strong alone, any other number only beside the name). An ISIN, a SWIFT code, a ticker, a national ID number, a passport number, a date, a gender, a title or an address is never a key.
  - **The record and what it leaves out.** The text begins `US Consolidated Screening List (trade.gov).` and holds the list, the name, other names (6 shown: the file's own, then the names that the `Issuer Name` ids of an entry give), countries (6), the programs, the websites (5 shown) and the registration numbers (6 shown) that the record is matched by, the day listed, for the Denied Persons List the day the order ends, the Federal Register notice (200 characters), the licence requirement and the licence policy (300 each) and the remarks (400), and, when the name has more than one entry on the list, how many (the other fields and the match keys are then those of the entry with the newest start date); a cut ends in ` [shortened]`, and the summary is at most 4,000 characters. It ends with the sentence that the list is an aid to screening and not the official list, and that the entry is to be checked against the official publication of the list (the Federal Register for the lists of Commerce and State). The title is `Consolidated Screening List, <list>: <name>`, and the address is `https://www.trade.gov/consolidated-screening-list` with the list's short id and the compact name as the query parameters `list` and `entry`, so that no two records share one. **Never stored:** the street, city, state and postal code of an address, dates and places of birth, citizenships and nationalities, gender, title, passport and national ID numbers, the vessel fields, the file's hashed ids, `standard_order`, `search_performed_at` and `last_imported`. The remarks, the licence fields and the OFAC ids are free text that a model reads, so each is cleaned (compatibility forms folded, control, zero-width and bidirectional characters out, white space collapsed) and cut before the projection is returned, and the record goes through the same escaping and untrusted-data line as every source.
  - **Download and limits.** One file, 64 MiB at most (it is 34 MB), `application/json` and `application/octet-stream`, 180 s, a conditional request, and the final host must be `data.trade.gov` over https (for a 304 too) or the sync fails as `wrong_root`; ITA answers a HEAD request with 404, so the job makes a GET. `download` parses the text once and checks that it is the whole list (an object whose `results` and `sources_used` are arrays, with `offset` 0 and `total` equal to the number of results, since a paged answer is not the list), and returns one body: the kept entries, reduced to the fields above and sorted, with the version of each kept list. The hash that the sync compares therefore does not change with the time the file was made (`search_performed_at`) or the `last_imported` of a list, and the 34 MB text and its objects are not held once `download` has returned. At most 20,000 records are accepted (more is `wrong_root`); an entry of a kept list with no name, a name or other name of more than 500 characters, or a field of the wrong JSON type, is skipped and counted up to max(10, 1%) of the entries read, as for the CVE records, and past that the sync fails as `parse_errors`; the shrink check applies. The version of the copy is the newest `source_last_updated` day among the kept lists.
  - **Coverage.** The row of `csl` is as for the other lists. It is researched with legal and regulatory matters, which need no website, so a vendor with none is looked up in it by name. A deployment upgraded to this version has no copy until the first sync, which the backend queues at its start: until then the row is `failed` with no day of copy, and the screen lists the list under "Not checked".
- **The FDA recalls** (Q34, 2026-11-07; spec sections D2.20 and D4.13). `fda_device_recalls` copies the recalls of the devices that are on the FDA's list of AI-enabled medical devices (`fda_ai_devices`), from openFDA (`api.fda.gov`, the device recall endpoint), joined to that list by the K and PMA numbers of its submissions. No gate is involved. The category is AI-specific risks and the record type `ai_device_recall`, an event: the 24-month lookback applies by the earliest initiation day of the event, and the record is offered with it. The class is official, and a match by name is weak. The recall endpoint has no class field, so the class of a recall (Class I, II or III) comes from the FDA's device enforcement reports, which the connector reads for the class and the day of the classification and for nothing else (Q37, 2026-11-10); a record of an event that those reports give no class says that the FDA's recall page shows it.
  - **The download is several requests, and gives nothing until every one has succeeded.** (1) A probe of each endpoint, `device/recall.json?limit=1` and `device/enforcement.json?limit=1` (16 MiB, as a join answer, since one product can carry millions of characters of lot numbers; 30 s): its `meta.last_updated` must be a day and its `meta.results.total` above 10,000, or the sync fails as `wrong_root`. openFDA answers 404 when a search has no match, and the probes are what lets the join and the class stage read a 404 as "no match" and not as a moved endpoint. (2) The CSV of the FDA list of AI-enabled devices, unconditionally (141 KB; the limits, the host pin to `www.fda.gov` and the header check of `fda.ts`). (3) The composite validator, below. (4) The join: the K numbers of the list (`K` and six digits, as written) in batches of 50 and its PMA numbers (`P` and six digits as written, and a supplement `P…/S…` with the slash removed, since openFDA writes it without one) in batches of 30, as `search=<field>:(<n>+OR+<n>…)&limit=1000` (without `+OR+` the parentheses mean AND and nothing matches), each request 16 MiB, 60 s, to `api.fda.gov` only. More than 1,000 results for a batch are paged with `skip` (5 pages at most; more is `wrong_root`, and so is a page that is not the last and holds fewer than 1,000 results), and a batch whose answer is cut at the limit is split in half and asked again, down to 5 numbers. A 404 on the first page of a batch is no match for that batch, and a 404 on a later page fails the sync as `wrong_root` (the search did not page as it was asked); any other failure fails the sync, a 429 with its `Retry-After`. De Novo numbers (`DEN…`) are not searched, and the base number of a supplement is not searched either: a recalled product is joined to a row of the list only when one of its `k_numbers` or `pma_numbers` equals the row's term exactly, so a recall that cites only the base PMA number of an AI-listed supplement is not joined. The requests are 500 ms apart (about 120 a minute, half of openFDA's limit of 240), and a download makes at most 80 requests and takes at most 96 MiB of answers (more is `too_large`); the list of 2026-11-07 takes 36 before the class stage (the two probes, the CSV and 33 batches: 32 of K numbers and one of PMA numbers) and 43 in all. (5) The class stage: the distinct recall numbers of the joined products (`product_res_number`), 50 a request, each between quotes, as `search=recall_number:(…+OR+…)` on `device/enforcement.json`, one page of 1,000 at most. A recall has one report, so an answer of more than a page, or whose total is not the number of its results, is not the list (`wrong_root`); a 404 is "no report" for the batch, and an answer that is too large is split in half as in the join. Of a report the connector reads three fields and keeps two: the class, which must be exactly `Class I`, `Class II` or `Class III`, and the day it was classified (`center_classification_date`, eight digits that form a day). It never reads the status, because the FDA does not update it after the classification and openFDA says the data is no way to follow a recall. A report with another class gives no class and counts as a row that cannot be read; a report with a class whose day is missing or is no day keeps its class, has no day and counts as nothing, so that the record says that the classification day is not stated. Of two reports of one number the one kept is decided by their values and never by their place in the answer: the more serious class (a report with no class is the least), and of one class the earlier day (none is the latest). A number counts as one row that cannot be read only when none of its reports has a class. (6) Each answer is projected at once to the fields of the record, and the rest, `code_info` first, is dropped before the next request, so that what the sync hashes and stores never holds it.
  - **How the sync decides that nothing changed.** A list has one `etag` and one `last_modified` in `vrdb_sources`, and this list is not one file. After the two probes and the CSV, the download builds `lastModified` as `recalls:<meta.last_updated>;enforcement:<meta.last_updated>;list:<the first 16 hexadecimal digits of the SHA-256 of the CSV text>` and leaves `etag` null (the column is TEXT, so the string is stored as it is). When that equals the stored value the download ends as `unchanged` and neither the join nor the class stage is run: three requests. A value stored in the form of version 1 of the connector, which has no enforcement day, never equals the new one, so the join runs again once after the upgrade (the connector's `version` is 2, and the fold of the enforcement reports did not raise it again, since nothing had been released). openFDA updates the dataset about weekly and the FDA list changes now and then, so the join, about 18 MB of answers because openFDA has no field projection and `code_info` can be megabytes, runs about once a week and not every day, which would spend 42 of the 1,000 requests a day that one IP address gets (the two probes, 33 batches and 7 requests of the class stage on 2026-11-07). An `unchanged` run counts as a success, so the copy is stale only after 48 hours without a sync.
  - **The record and what it leaves out.** One record per recall event (`res_event_number`, digits, 12 at most), whatever the number of products, since one firm can have dozens in one event. The text begins `FDA medical device recalls (openFDA), joined to the FDA list of AI-enabled medical devices.` and holds the event number, the recalling firms (3), the days initiated (the earliest of the products), posted (the earliest) and terminated (the latest, only when every product has ended), the status (the most open of the products), the root cause as the FDA records it (3 values at most), the reason for the recall (that of the first product, by recall number, that has one; 600 characters at most), the products (their number and 3 descriptions of 200 characters at most), the devices of the FDA list that the recall covers (3 at most, each with its submission number and company), the recall numbers (5 at most), the FDA's class of the event (the most serious class that any of its products has, Class I before II before III, how many of its products have it unless all do, and the earliest day the FDA classified any of those, or that the classification day is not stated when none of them has a day, from the enforcement reports; when no product has a class, the sentence that the FDA's recall page shows it), and the closing sentence that a recall is a firm's correction or removal of a device, classified by the FDA, not a finding that the device's AI caused the problem, and that openFDA says its results are unvalidated and not to be relied on for decisions about medical care. A cut ends in ` [shortened]`, and the summary is at most 4,000 characters. The recalling firm is not always the vendor (a subsidiary, a distributor), so the text names both the firm and the company of the FDA list, and both are name keys (weak, 50 at most for a record). The address is the FDA's recall page of the event. **Never stored:** `code_info`, the addresses of the firm, `additional_info_contact` (a named contact and a phone number), `distribution_pattern`, `product_quantity`, `action`, `firm_fei_number`, `cfres_id`, the `openfda` block (its GMDN content is licensed separately), the `k_numbers` and `pma_numbers` that were not joined, and every field of an enforcement report but its class and the day it was classified (the class is also an identifier of the record). `reason_for_recall` and `product_description` are free text that a model reads, so they are cleaned (control, zero-width and bidirectional characters out, white space collapsed), cut, and go through the same escaping and untrusted-data line as every source.
  - **Limits.** At most 5,000 events (more is `wrong_root`), 20 recall and submission numbers in the identifiers, and a recall number of the form `Z-NNNN-NN` (the years 2002 to 2006) or `Z-NNNN-NNNN`; a product with another number, or an initiation day that is not a day, is skipped and counted, with the tolerance of max(10, 1%) of the CVE records, and a report of the class stage whose class cannot be read is counted the same way, as one row and no more, since its product is one already (`skipped` counts the products left out, `classSkipped` the recall numbers none of whose reports has a class; a day that cannot be read counts as nothing). The shrink check applies to the events (155 in the live run of 2026-11-07).
  - **Coverage.** The category is AI-specific risks, which a run researches only for a vendor with a website: for a vendor with none the row is `not_researched`, and the list is on no "Checked" or "Not checked" line. A deployment upgraded to this version has no copy until the first sync, which the backend queues at its start: until then the row is `failed` with no day of copy, under "Not checked".
- **The third round of lists** (Q35 to Q53, 2026-11-10; spec sections D2.21 to D2.43 and D4.14). On 2026-11-10 the product owner asked for every list of the research of 2026-11-09 that can be added at once, and delegated the decisions (see **The gates** above). Twenty-three lists were built, all open: 22 are copies and GLEIF is read through. The FDA device enforcement reports were folded into the FDA recalls (Q37, above), and eight lists of the research were not built (D2.44). The source registry stays at `sr-6`, since no host is added. `VRDB_SOURCES` holds the 23 after the FDA recalls in the order of the table above, and a run offers the records in that order: within a category the sanctions entries come before the enforcement entries and those before the product safety entries, and the lists that can raise a risk before the registers.
  - **The lists, by category.** Legal and regulatory matters (`legal_regulatory`, 10): Canada's sanctions list (`canada_sema`); the enforcement and case lists of the UK OFSI (`uk_ofsi`), the FTC (`ftc_penalties`, `ftc_competition`), the European Commission (`eu_dg_comp`), the UK CMA (`uk_cma`) and Colombia's SIC (`co_sic_penalties`); and the product safety lists of NHTSA (`nhtsa_recalls`, `nhtsa_odi`) and the UK MHRA (`uk_mhra`). Security incidents (`security_incidents`, 2): the California breach notices (`california_breaches`) and the disclose.io program list (`disclose_io`). AI-specific risks (`ai_specific`, 8): the AI incident lists (`charlotin`, `piperoll`) and six registers (`canada_ai_register`, `omb_ai_inventory`, `nl_algoritmeregister`, `uk_atrs`, `epoch_ai`, `fmti`). Certifications (`certifications`, 2): `fedramp` and `c2pa_conforming`. Financial health (`financial_health`, 1): `gleif`. Eight copies run every day (`canada_sema`, `california_breaches`, `charlotin`, `piperoll`, `nhtsa_recalls`, `nhtsa_odi`, `fedramp`, `c2pa_conforming`) and 14 once a week, each at a minute of its own, away from the hours 0, 6, 12 and 18 of the six-hourly lists; a copy of a weekly list is stale after 192 hours and of a daily one after 48. A run researches certifications and AI-specific risks only for a vendor with a website, so for a vendor with none the lists of those two categories are `not_researched`.
  - **The list kit** (`vrdb/connectors/kit/`). The 22 copies of 2026-11-10 are specs of one factory (`createFileConnector` in `kit/fileConnector.ts`): where the files are, how to read them and what a row becomes, and the factory does the rest. It asks the files in order through the sync's fetch, and the answer to each, a 304 included, must come from the pinned host and directory after the redirects (`kit/address.ts`), or the download fails as not allowed; a file cut at its limit fails as too large; and what the reader does not take for the list fails as not the list, as does any other error of a reader (no error holds an address, a row or a word of the file). A list of one file is asked with the validators of the last download, and a list of several files is asked in full every time, since a 304 on one file would say nothing of the others, and the sync's hash of the body tells an unchanged list. The etag that a snapshot stores is `k<version>:<the server's etag>`, and the validators are sent only when the stored etag has the prefix of the version that reads now, so that the first download after a bump of `version` reads the file again. The connectors of 2026-11-10 are at `version` 1, since none of them had run against a stored snapshot before its first release. A read that is partial is no read: a reader says whether its rows are the whole list (`complete`), and one that stopped at its limit of rows, at a quote that was never closed or at a row of too many columns fails the download as too large and never gives a shorter list, which would mark every record that it lacks as gone. The body is the rows sorted by the spec's key and then by their canonical text, so that the order of a file decides nothing. The records are built one row at a time by `recordOf` (`kit/record.ts`), the one place where a record is made: an id of more than 200 characters is no id, a title is cut at 200 characters and a summary at 1,300 (the cut says so, with ` [shortened]`), no `<` or `>` is left in a title, a summary, an identifier or a key (they are written as the angle quotation marks), the phrase "lookup note" is written "lookup-note" in a title and a summary, and a name, an LEI or a number is folded like a cell before it is a key. A text that must be a name, a key or an id is read with `lineWithin` (`kit/clean.ts`), which gives a clean line that fits or nothing and never a cut one, and the keys refuse any text that ends in the cut marker, so that no cut text is ever matched; the ids, identifiers and displays are each connector's to keep uncut. The rows that cannot be read, and the entries that the builder refuses, are counted against an allowance of ten or a hundredth of the rows when that is more, and never more than a tenth; beyond it the parse fails as a new format. A reader that groups raw rows into entities gives a `total` that is the larger of the raw rows and the entities plus the unreadable ones, and one that counts its unreadable ones in raw lines (the two NHTSA files) says so with `allowanceBasis`. The other files of the kit are `clean.ts` (lines, days, angle brackets), `table.ts`, `json.ts`, `text.ts` and `stream.ts` (the readers of hostile text, which read once from the front and stop at a limit), `archives.ts` (one file of a zip, one sheet of a workbook), `paging.ts` (the several requests of one list, each pinned), `summary.ts` (a few sentences and a closing sentence that is never cut), `persons.ts` and `pause.ts`.
  - **How a record begins.** The text of every record begins with the list's mark, the operator's own host in parentheses (never the host of a file, a CDN or a storage bucket: `nhtsa.gov`, `international.gc.ca`, `github.com/FedRAMP`, `gov.uk`) and the kind of list, as in `NHTSA defect investigations (nhtsa.gov), a product safety list.` The kinds are `a sanctions list`, `an enforcement or case list`, `a product safety list`, `an AI incident list`, `a data breach notice list` and `a register or status list`, and the analysis prompt names each list by its mark (`<ID>_RECORD_MARK`, exported by its connector). No mark is the prefix of another.
  - **Events and states.** The 12 lists that record matters are events. Their record types are `breach_notice_summary`, `sanctions_penalty_notice`, `hallucination_cases`, `civil_penalty_summary`, `agent_incidents`, `eu_competition_cases`, `competition_case_summary`, `competition_action_summary`, `consumer_sanction_summary`, `vehicle_recall_summary`, `field_safety_notice_summary` and `defect_investigations`, all in `EVENT_RECORD_TYPES` of `vrdb/lookup.ts`: each is dated by its newest matter, the lookback of the category applies to that day (36 months for legal and regulatory matters, 24 months for security incidents and AI-specific risks), and the record is offered with it. A party of `uk_cma` with an open case has no day, and the lookback lets it through. The other 11 are states, offered as of the day of the copy, with no lookback: `sanctions_entity` (Canada's sanctions list, as for the other sanctions lists), `algorithm_register_supplier` (shared on purpose by `uk_atrs`, `canada_ai_register`, `omb_ai_inventory` and `nl_algoritmeregister`), `ai_model_developer`, `transparency_index_score`, `c2pa_conformance`, `cloud_authorization_status`, `disclosure_program` and `lei_record`. Every type is at most 30 characters, as `record_type` is `VARCHAR(30)` (the longest are `algorithm_register_supplier` and `field_safety_notice_summary`, 27).
  - **Weak lists, and lists that give context only.** A list that can raise a risk is matched by name only, so its record is weak and carries the lookup note: the label is `low` at most. For the AI incident, enforcement and case, product safety and breach lists, prompt `vr-15` lets an entry raise at most one risk, in the category of its list, worded "An entity named … appears in …" (for the California list, "An entity named … reported a data breach to the California Attorney General"), with a likelihood of Possible at most and an action plan that is a check of who the vendor is against the entry; Canada's sanctions list joins the sanctions paragraph of the prompt and is worded as the other sanctions lists. Eight lists were built weak, because their match is by name only and their identifiers are thin or a name is taken from a title: `uk_atrs`, `uk_cma`, `uk_ofsi`, `uk_mhra`, `piperoll`, `charlotin`, `nl_algoritmeregister` and `ftc_competition`. Ten lists give context only (`CONTEXT_ONLY_RECORD_SOURCES` in `records/types.ts`): `canada_ai_register`, `omb_ai_inventory`, `nl_algoritmeregister`, `uk_atrs`, `epoch_ai`, `fmti`, `c2pa_conforming`, `fedramp`, `disclose_io` and `gleif`; two of them, `uk_atrs` and `nl_algoritmeregister`, are also built weak, so only the other six built-weak lists raise a risk. The prompt tells the model that an entry of a register or status list raises no risk, whatever it says, that a vendor missing from one is no risk either, and that the entry may only be used as context in the description of another risk, worded as the register's own statement. The code backs that twice: the evidence pack gives such records up before any other source, and they never support a credibility label (see [Credibility](#credibility)). Context only is not name only: `fedramp` and `disclose_io` carry a domain key (strong against a verified domain), `uk_atrs` a Companies House number and `gleif` an LEI, and a strong record of such a list is still no support for a label.
  - **Natural persons.** A party that is a natural person is dropped before anything is stored, by rules that were each measured on their own list, with their word lists copied as they are and not merged (`connectors/kit/persons.ts`): the FTC penalties (`hasLegalForm`, `looksLikePerson`; its precision is low, so a firm whose name has the shape of a person's, two capitalized words and no legal form, is dropped as well), the EU competition cases (`euOrgEvidence`: a cartel case and a DMA case keep every name, and in any other antitrust case a name is kept only when it shows an organization or occurs in two cases or more, so that a person named in a cartel or DMA case, or in two cases, stays), the Colombian sanctions (`looksLikePersonEs`, asked of the whole cell and then of each side of it) and the California breach notices (`californiaPracticeRule`, a practice named for a person, and `CALIFORNIA_MIDDLE_INITIAL_NAME`; a few plain two-word names of persons are not caught, and the record of one is offered only to a vendor that has exactly that name). Every rule fails closed, a company being lost rather than a person kept, and a name that a rule drops is never an id, a key, a display, a title, a part of the text or an identifier, and is counted nowhere. Four rules stay in their connectors, because only one list needs each: the shape of a person's name in the OFSI notices, the honorifics of the CMA cases (Mr, Mrs, Ms and Miss, with or without a full stop: a party of that form is dropped, and its case stays for the other parties), the terms that make a C2PA applicant the business of a person and the academic titles of the Dutch register. What the main rules read, and what each leaves open, is described in the paragraphs that follow this one. Canada's sanctions list tells individuals from entities by its own elements (a record that has an element that only an individual or a ship has, such as a given name, a family name, a ship's number or a date of birth, is skipped before any other cell of it is read). Where the field that would name a person is never read, no rule is needed: the AI Hallucination Cases database reads free text in its tool cell only, every distinct value of which was read when the list was designed and none is a person, and the explanation of a vendor's dispute only for which tools it names, never keeping it, and never its case names, pointers, sources, details or items; PipeRoll takes no full name (an operator that is an individual is out by its category, the first party of a case caption, the text before the first `v`, `v.`, `vs` or `vs.` that has a blank on each side, in any letter case, is dropped, a name is one token with no blank, and a word that has the shape of a surname with a prefix or of an account handle is never a name; its residuals are below); the UK transparency records are read in their section of the supplier and their section of the Companies House number only, never in the named officials and contact data of the rest of the text (no person rule is applied to the supplier section, so a supplier named by a person's name, a sole trader, is kept as written); and the authors of Epoch AI's models, its Hugging Face developer id and the contact fields of the lists that have them are never read. Other lists apply no filter, each for a reason its connector states: the Government of Canada AI Register has no rule that tells a person from a company in its vendor cell (the best was one hit in five), so the residual under one percent is accepted; the filers and manufacturers of the two NHTSA files are businesses (every name of the real files was read when the lists were designed, and a rule by the shape of a name had no precision); the US federal AI use case inventory has no natural person among the strings of its vendor cells that were read when the list was designed, and none is kept apart; the MHRA lists name the manufacturers of medical devices, which are companies, and a rule of honorifics would only flag a company named after a person; the organization cell of Epoch AI's models names organizations (every token of the real file was read when the list was designed, and none was a person) and the column headers of the Foundation Model Transparency Index are the brand names of organizations; the FedRAMP file names companies, its only persons being the contacts of the assessors and the mailboxes, which are never read; the disclose.io list names organizations and products, its only individuals being customers' or users' own subdomains on hosting platforms, which sit in the two scope entries that are never read; and the FTC competition list has no field but a matter title, so it cuts the parts of a title where a natural person is named (every parenthetical group and everything after the first hyphen with a blank on each side) and applies no person rule, since none was found that works on a title, so that a person named in the party part of a title would be a party. A GLEIF record is the vendor's own, by its stored LEI, and holds no address, so no filter by the kind of entity is applied.
  - **Natural persons: the FTC penalties.** The names of the defendants are the one place where a person can be named. Every parenthetical group of a case name is cut before anything else is read (individual defendants and brands are written there), except a nickname, and the party rule is asked of what is left: a party that is a person's name is dropped, with its row's share of the case, before anything is stored. The kit's rule has a low precision, so a firm whose name has the shape of a person's is dropped as well. The connector also closes shapes that the rule lets through, each failing closed: a group after a comma that begins with `and`, `&` or `+` goes on with the group before it; the `Last, First` shape drops the whole party, and with a surname that is no bare word (two to four words, a suffix such as `Jr.` or `III`, a particle or `Estate of`) the person's group, the given name that follows it and everything after the given name go, while what stands before the person stays; the particles `de`, `del`, `la`, `las`, `los`, `el`, `en` and the `y` are taken out of a group before the rule judges it; `doing business as`, `trading as` and `t/a` split a name as `d/b/a` does, so that the person before them is dropped and the business after them stays; a group of legal wording alone (`individually`, `jointly and severally`) is no party; a name that goes on under another name (`a/k/a`, `aka`, `f/k/a`, `n/k/a`) is the same defendant's, and when every principal party is dropped only the business that a `d/b/a`, `dba`, `t/a`, `doing business as` or `trading as` names is promoted; for the person shape only (the stored text is never changed), every quotation mark of Unicode is read as a quotation mark and every dash as a hyphen; the head of a name ends at a dash that separates (a blank, one or two dashes of any script, a blank); a nickname in parentheses, brackets or braces (one or two capitalized words or initials) is left out of what the rule judges, and a name with a parenthesis is judged as it was always read and as it is named, a group staying only if both readings keep it; and a piece that holds `Estate of`, or a name of more than one word that ends in `Estate`, is a person's. The costs of these rules, which are accepted: a firm named as two one-word brands in a row, a one-word brand that stands before a person, a firm of one word, a parenthesis and a word with no legal form, a firm that holds `Estate of`, and a US place name that begins with a Spanish particle and has no legal form are dropped. Left open, and stored as the rules give it: a person's name of five words or more once its particles are out; a person's name with a digit; a join with no blank round it; a `y` between a company with a legal form and a person, since the legal form wins; a person whose surname is a word of the kit's lists (an organization word or a legal form); the words `with`, `et`, `und` and `e`, which join nothing; a trust that names its trustee or is named after a person, which is stored; a parenthesis that is no nickname, which is cut as ever so that its head is stored; a nickname inside the name of a company, which stores the first word as the entity; a bracket or a brace that holds a name after a company or an alias mark, which stays in the name; a run of three dashes, or a dash that touches the word after it; a colon, a vertical bar, a tilde, a bullet, a middle dot or the CJK corner brackets between a company and a person; and estate wordings other than the two above. On the real file of 2026-11-10 the closures lose one record: a firm named as a list behind a group of a person's shape, lost to the given-name rule.
  - **Natural persons: the Colombian sanctions.** About a quarter of the rows fine a person, and one cell may name several parties. The Spanish rule takes no cell that holds a `/` or an `&`, or a company beside a person, for a person's name as a whole, so the connector judges the cleaned cell twice before anything is stored. As a whole, a cell with the shape of a person's name drops its row. Side by side, the cell is split on ` Y/O `, on a hyphen or a dash with a blank on one side at least, on a slash, and on ` Y `, ` E `, ` & ` and ` + ` with a blank on each side, and each side is judged on its own without its tails (`EN LIQUIDACION`, `CON SIGLA`), since a company has them and an organization word in them would hide the shape of a person's name. A side with the shape of a person's name is removed, tails and all; when none is left the row is dropped, and when some are the party of the row is the sides that remain, joined by ` Y/O `, and the cell that names the person is never a name, an id, a display, a title, a part of the text, an identifier or a key. When sides were removed, the row is made only if a remaining side can be matched by a vendor (four letters and digits once its legal form is off, and a word that is no legal form, no word of an organization, no particle and no word of a family firm): a person beside `HERMANOS`, `LTDA`, `CIA S EN C` or `HIJOS` alone leaves no company, and the row is dropped, counted nowhere. The rule is asked of a text whose typographic apostrophes are made ASCII and whose trailing parenthesis or number of identity (`C.C.`, `CC`, `NIT` and its number) is taken off, since what follows a person's name holds words that no name has. What the rule does not see stays in the party: a name with a letter that Spanish does not use, a name of more than six words (the particles of Spanish count as words), the compound surname of a person that holds a ` Y `, and a person behind a hyphen or a dash with no blank on either side, a parenthesis or a comma, which join nothing, a side of one word, and a person's name that holds an organization word. A company of two plain words that ends in a parenthesis reads as a person's with the parenthesis off, and its row is lost; no cell of the real list of 2026-11-10 is one.
  - **Natural persons: the California breach notices.** The two rules (`californiaPracticeRule`: a leading Dr., a leading Law Office or Law Offices of, a credential after the last comma or as the last word of a name of three words or more; `CALIFORNIA_MIDDLE_INITIAL_NAME`: first name, middle initial, last name) read a name as a whole, and a person can be named in a part of one, so each part is judged on its own before the name is used: the parts that an alias mark sets apart, the text inside each pair of parentheses and the text outside them. A part with the shape of a person is cut out of the name with the mark or the parentheses that held it, the spelling of the row is what remains, and the name as it was written is never an id, a display, a title, a part of the text, an identifier or a key; when nothing remains, the row is dropped, like the row of a person, and counted nowhere. Brackets that do not pair up one to one cannot be taken apart with certainty, so a person between any two of them takes the whole part with it, and the text of a pair is judged as it is and again with the alias marks that open it taken off, so that a mark never hides a person. A person's practice written as the pieces of one name, behind a comma and a blank or an ampersand with a blank on each side, is dropped when a piece that remains has the shape of a person and no word of the name is a legal form or a word of an organization; a family firm that carries one is kept, which is the cost of the rule. What the rules do not see stays in the name: the middle-initial rule reads ASCII letters only and the whole name from its first letter to its last, so a name with an accented letter, in lower case, behind a title such as `Mr.`, with a particle, last name first behind a comma, or with a possessive is no person's by it; an alias mark written another way (`d.b.a.`, `dba:`, `doing business as`, `t/a`) splits nothing (six names of the list of 2026-11-10 hold one); a comma or an ampersand with no blank, `and` and a hyphen join nothing; and a few plain two-word names of persons are not caught, so that the record of one is offered only to a vendor that has exactly that name.
  - **Natural persons: the Dutch register.** The one rule is the academic title or honorific, which marks a natural person; the other rule that was tried, two or three capitalized words, found no person in 22 hits and is not applied, and the 4 provider cells (3 distinct texts) of the real file that carried a title (counted on 2026-11-10) were all persons. A title is a word at the start of a name or after a blank, a colon, a semicolon, a comma or a bracket (a square one too), in any case and whatever follows it: `prof`, `dr`, `drs`, `ir`, `mr`, `dhr`, `mevr` and `mevrouw`, and `ing` and `bc` with a full stop (a bare `ING` is the bank). A post-nominal after a name (`MSc`, `MBA`, `PhD`, `LLM` and `RA`, with or without the full stops) is a marker too, as a whole word in the piece or as the text of brackets; a name that begins with the letters of one (`MSC Zorbex`) is a company's. A title that follows the name it qualifies is the same person: brackets whose text begins with a title drop the piece they belong to, and a piece that begins with a title, or has such brackets, drops the piece before it when a comma separated the two (the surname of a name written last name first); a title that stands after initials, or after more pieces, takes with it the comma-separated pieces before it that are initials or one capitalized word, back to a piece that holds a legal form or an organization word, has two capitalized words or a lower-case word, or that another separator set apart. A semicolon, a slash and the word `en` separate two parties, so that a company beside a person stays. The rule fails closed: a company whose name starts with a title word, is followed by a post-nominal or ends in `LLM`, `MBA` or `RA` is dropped as a person is, and a company in front of a title behind a comma is lost with it, since a comma is also what separates a surname from its title. Left open: a title glued to initials, a name with a particle or of two capitalized words before a title, a surname or an initial in lower case, a title in later brackets or after other words of a piece that does not begin with one, a hyphenated initial, `mw.`, `t.a.v.` before a person, the post-nominals `BSc`, `MA`, `MD`, `RC`, `RE` and `EMIA`, and every person with no title at all.
  - **Natural persons: the C2PA list.** The kit has no rule for this list, and the connector applies its own. A product whose applicant holds one of the terms that make it the business of a person is dropped as soon as its applicant is read, whatever else is wrong with it, and counts nowhere, not even as unreadable. The terms are tested in any case: `sole propriet` and `sole trader`, `einzelunternehm`, `einzelkaufmann` and `einzelkauffrau`, `freelanc` and `self employed` (in the terms of two words the two words may stand apart by up to three blanks or dashes of any kind), the German `e.K`, `e. K`, `e.Kfm` and `e.Kfr` and the Austrian `e.U.` as words of their own, `Einzelfirma`, `eenmanszaak`, `entreprise individuelle`, `EIRL`, `auto-entrepreneur`, `micro-entrepreneur`, `empresario individual`, `autonomo`, `ditta individuale`, `enskild firma` and `enkeltpersonforetak` as whole words, and `Sole Prop.` and `Inh.`; `EI`, of two letters, is no term. The same terms keep an organization name out of the keys, and a company whose name holds one is dropped too. A person who trades under a business name is that business: in an applicant `<name>, dba <business>` (also `d/b/a`, `doing business as`, `t/a` and `trading as`) the part before the first mark is cut out of everything the record holds when it holds a letter or a digit and no legal form and no word of an organization (the words that join the parts of a name, such as `and` and `of`, are no organization words), and a mark with nothing readable after it leaves a person's business, which is dropped. Left open: a sole trader who does not say so, or says it in a form that is not listed (four separators between the words, a minus sign for a dash); the other forms of the marks (`dba:`, `d.b.a.`, a mark inside brackets, `operating as`, `o/a`, `trading under`) and the reverse order `<business> dba <person>`; and a certificate organization that is only the person's name, or `<business> (<person>)`, which is still read as a second name of the record after the applicant is renamed to its business, so that the person's name can reach the match keys, the stored row and, through a product or certificate name, the summary (in the real file of 2026-11-10 the three applicants with a mark all have an organization that carries the mark and is cut, so that no person's name survives). The list is context only, and no record of it raises a risk.
  - **Natural persons: PipeRoll.** A name comes from the operator cell, when the registry says that the operator is an enterprise or a startup (an individual is out by its category), and from the words of a title that have a lower-case letter followed later by a capital and no digit. Such a word is never a name when it has the shape of a surname with a prefix (`Mc`, `Mac`, `De`, `Van`, `Von`, `Fitz`, `St` and the like, then one or more capitalized parts, with or without one capital glued in front as the initial of a given name) or of an account handle (three or more lower-case letters before the first capital), whoever it names: no list of company names is kept, and a company that the operator cell names still has its key. A word that a letter touches (an accented letter, a letter of another script) is the stub of a longer word and is never a name, and only the part before a hyphen or an apostrophe gives a name. In a case caption the text before the first separator is the first party, a natural person in every caption of the real file, and it is dropped. The title of each of the newest entries that a record quotes goes through the same shapes: the first party of a caption is quoted as "A party" and each person-shaped part of a word as "[name]". A title that was cut is read, and quoted, without its last word, which may be the fragment of a name. Left open, quoted as written (and a key when the word has the brand shape of the title rule): a person in the second position of a caption; a person whose name has neither shape (an intercapped given name, a short lower-case run before a capital, a handle written with capitals, a lower-case letter or two capitals glued in front of a surname); the other part of a double-barrelled name of which one part only has a shape; a surname that apostrophes cut in the middle of its own parts; digits inside a word; a handle or a surname written wholly in letters that the ASCII reader does not see (accented letters, Cyrillic), which is neither quoted as a name nor a key; a caption written with another word (`against`, `&`, `versus`) or with no blank on a side; and the first party of a caption whose separator the cut of a title to 300 characters reached or went past. A product that is written like a handle (`macOS`, `openAI`) is quoted as "[name]" and gives no key.
  - **Natural persons: a residual of the kit.** The kit's English and Spanish shapes (`looksLikePerson` and `looksLikePersonEs`) read the ASCII hyphen, the ASCII apostrophe and the ASCII separators, so a name written with U+2010, U+2011, U+2212, the en dash, U+2018 or the middle dot U+00B7 is not read as a person by them. The FTC penalties connector maps every dash and quotation mark of Unicode to its ASCII form before it asks the rule (for the person shape only), and the Colombian connector maps the typographic apostrophes (U+2018 and U+2019); the other marks stay a residual of the Colombian rule and, for the middle dot, of the FTC rule. The FTC penalties workbook and the Colombian data of 2026-11-10 hold none of these characters. The remedy, if a list ever shows one, is to map the Unicode hyphens and quotation marks to ASCII before the rule, which fails closed.
  - **Record addresses are unique.** `WebSession.addRecords` drops a record whose address is already in the run's ledger, and the provenance and the verifier map a record by its address, so two records that shared one would lose the second. Most lists therefore build an address for each entity as a query on the page that describes the list, as `fda.ts` and `csl.ts` do (the last column of the table gives each address). Six lists whose first design gave every record the same page were changed to do so: `?tool=` for `charlotin`, `?developer=` for `fmti`, `?vendor=` for `omb_ai_inventory` and `canada_ai_register`, `?organisation=` for `epoch_ai` and `?applicant=` for `c2pa_conforming`. The GOV.UK lists and `piperoll` point at the newest item's own page and offer one record. `ftc_competition` points at the page of the newest matter and offers two, so that two parties of one matter share an address and the second record is dropped as a duplicate of the same matter, which is accepted.
  - **Pauses between requests, and robots.txt.** A list is read without `robots.txt`, as a file that its operator publishes for download (`connectorFetch` in `vrdb/worker.ts`); the connectors keep the operators' Crawl-delay between their own requests through `connectors/kit/pause.ts` (`HOST_PAUSE_MS`): 20 seconds for `open.canada.ca` (`canada_ai_register`: the dataset record, then the file), 5 seconds for `www.ftc.gov` (`ftc_penalties`: `data.json`, then the workbook), 1 second for `www.datos.gov.co` (`co_sic_penalties`: the count, then the rows), 1 second for the bucket of the Commission's open data on Amazon S3 (`eu_dg_comp`: the antitrust file, then the Digital Markets Act file, with the pause once between them and never before the first request; the bucket gives no Crawl-delay, so the house pause of one second is used) and 400 milliseconds between two pages of the GOV.UK Search API (`govukSearchPages` in `connectors/govukSearch.ts`, at most three requests a second, for `uk_ofsi`, `uk_cma`, `uk_mhra` and `uk_atrs`). `oag.ca.gov` asks for 10 seconds between requests and gets one request a sync. A connector that makes several requests is built by a factory that takes the sleep, as `fdaRecalls.ts` does, so that its tests do not wait; the pauses are timers and do not block the worker. The GOV.UK pager takes the pages as one search only when every page states the same total and the links of the results add up to it, and any failed page fails the download, so that a list taken in part, or shifted by a case published between two requests, never reaches the copy. On a deployment the first syncs of the 22 copies run one after another, in the order of the table, which keeps the lists of one host apart.
  - **The NHTSA flat files are streamed.** `nhtsa_recalls` and `nhtsa_odi` each read one zip whose single entry is a tab-delimited text with no header row: 312.8 MB inflated and 247,176 lines for the recalls (29 fields), 390.6 MB and 154,460 lines for the investigations (11 fields), observed on 2026-11-10. `connectors/nhtsaFlat.ts` asks for the zip with one conditional request (at most 64 MiB for the recalls and 32 MiB for the investigations), checks its directory (exactly one deflated entry, no Zip64) and streams the entry through `streamZipEntry` of `vrdb/zip.ts` and the kit's `LineSplitter`, in pieces of 64 KiB, so that the entry is never held in memory; the size that the directory declares is checked against a cap before a byte is read, and the bytes are counted against it as they come.
