So a page asked for on the vendor's domain that redirected off it does not count as the vendor's own, and has to name the vendor like any other page. A page about a company with a similar name cannot get in.
- **The quote says more than the name.** A citation is also removed unless its quote has at least one word that is no part of the vendor's own words. A word is a run of letters and digits in any script, compared after the quote is normalised as for the verbatim check. The vendor's own words are those of its name and of its domain; `www`, `http` and `https` (a page writes the domain with them, and the vendor record does not keep them); and the possessive of each word of the name and of the domain, which is the word followed by `s`, because the apostrophe is removed before words are compared ("Acme's" is "acmes"). For the vendor Acme Analytics (`acme-analytics.com`), the quotes "Acme", "Acme Analytics", "acme-analytics.com", "www.acme-analytics.com", "https://acme-analytics.com" and "Acme's" do not count, while "Acme confirmed the incident", "SOC 2 Type II" and "ISO 27001" do. This is not a minimum length. A refused quote does not use up its source: a later quote of the same source can still count. So a second hostname whose only quote is the vendor's name or domain no longer lifts a finding to `high`. There is no list of words, so any quote with one word beyond these still counts, and this is not a closed list of exceptions: a company suffix ("Acme Analytics Inc"), a path after the domain ("https://acme-analytics.com/trust") and, for a vendor with no domain on record, the top-level domain ("acme-analytics.com") all still count.
- **Candidates.** A candidate is dropped when no citation is left, when its category has no source in the evidence pack, when a text is empty or longer than 300 characters, when an enum value is not valid, or when `calculateRiskLevel(risk_severity, likelihood)` gives no level. The stored `risk_level` is that level followed by ` Risk`.
- **No confidence.** The validation sets no ceiling and stores no confidence: the label of a kept finding is computed afterwards, from the sources it cites, by `credibilityOf` (see [Credibility](#credibility)). The confidence ceiling that the earlier rules set from the tier of the cited hosts (`sourceTiers.ts`) is gone with the flag; a publisher is now read from the registry (`sourceRegistry/publisher.ts`: the name of the site without its top-level domain, so one story on `bbc.co.uk` and `bbc.com` is one publisher, and an aggregator is no publisher at all).
- **Clean text.** The texts the model wrote (the three finding texts, the rationale, the quotes and the category notes) are cleaned like the evidence is: control characters other than tab, line feed and carriage return are removed and lone surrogates are replaced. PostgreSQL refuses them, and one would fail the save of the whole run.

Candidates are ordered most severe first. What the checks cannot tell is a page that says something false about the vendor, or a quote from a page that gives orders to a model: both are real text of a page that names the vendor. The person who accepts a finding is the check for that.

### De-duplication

The register and the list should not collect the same risk again, and a dismissed finding should not come back on the next run. `dropDuplicates` (`dedupe.ts`) compares the words of the description and the impact together, using the Jaccard score of `services/vendors/textSimilarity.ts` (lower case, letters and digits, words of three characters or more):

| A candidate is compared with | It is dropped when |
|------------------------------|--------------------|
| The vendor's active register risks | The score is 0.25 or more |
| The vendor's earlier findings, whatever their status | The score is 0.4 or more, or it is in the same category and cites a URL that finding cited |
| A candidate of this run that was kept | The score is 0.4 or more (the first is kept) |
| The events of this run | Every source it cites is in an event that a kept candidate cites (see below) |

**One event is at most one finding.** The research finds sources one by one, and a model can turn one attack into three findings (what happened, what may have been taken, who was told), each in its own words. `clusterSources` (`clusters.ts`) finds the sources of the evidence pack that report the same event, with no model: two sources are one event when both have a date, the later is at most 21 days after the earliest of the event (counted from the first, so a run of weekly stories does not chain into one), and they are of one kind of event (an intrusion, an outage, a lawsuit or an enforcement, read from the title and the first 300 characters of the text; a page of the vendor's own site is read by its title alone), or when they name the same vulnerability (a `CVE-` id). A source with no date joins an event only through a CVE id. The analysis shows the model the sources of an event together, in an `<event id="E1">` element, and asks for the event to be one finding; the code then keeps the first, that is the most severe, candidate of an event and drops a later one that cites only sources of events a kept candidate cites (`same_event` in the trace as `duplicate.same_event`), also when it is in another category. A candidate that cites a source outside every event, or an event nobody cited, stays. Two incidents of one kind in the same three weeks are taken for one; that is the cost of a rule with no model.

`duplicates_dropped` on the run counts them. The caps (3 per category, 8 per run) are applied after the de-duplication, in severity order, so risks that are already known cannot take the places of new ones on a second run.

### The check of a finding that rests on snippets

A snippet is what a search engine shows of a page, cut, joined or reworded by the engine. The validation proves that the model copied the snippet right, not that the page says it. So once the caps have left the findings of a run (at most eight), `verifySnippetFindings` (`verifySnippets.ts`, called by `analyse` with the page reader of the research run) checks each finding whose cited sources are all search results, none of them a page that the research read:

1. It reads the page of the most credible cited source (by its registry category: official, trusted press, the vendor, unverified; of equals, the one the model cited first), and when that page cannot be read, the next one; two pages at most for a finding, one deadline of 20 seconds for all the reads, `robots.txt` and the guarded fetch as for every page the agent reads.
2. A page that holds the quote (compared like the validation does; the parts between the gap marks of an engine, `…` or `...`, each count) **confirms** the finding. It is stored as it was.
3. A page that does not hold the quote marks the finding `snippet_only`, and the credibility rule calls that source contradicted, so the finding is labelled `low`. The finding is kept.
4. When no page can be read the finding is marked `snippet_only` and its label is capped at `medium`: nothing was found against it, but nobody has seen the page either.

The list of findings shows a marked finding with the line "Not confirmed on the page. The quotes come from search results." under the caption of its sources. The reads are in the [trace](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-run-trace) of the run (phase `verification`, steps `by: "verification"` with the outcome `confirmed`, `not_on_page` or `failed`). The evaluation measures it as `snippetOnlyFindings`, looking the quotes up in the pages that the run read.

### The verifier

The verifier was told about databases in `vr-12`: on 2026-11-01 the first golden attempt saw it reject all five claims that rested on entries of the CISA catalog, because one quote of an entry cannot hold both the name of the catalog and the description of the flaw, and the verifier saw neither the database nor the whole record.

The checks above are made by code. What code cannot read is meaning: a source can name the vendor and be about another company with a similar name, a quote can be on the page and say less than the claim states, and a matter can be older than the lookback of its category when the source has no date to show it. So once the caps have left the findings of a run (at most eight), `verifierPasses` (`verifier.ts`, called by `analyse`) makes **one** more model call about all of them, with the model of the analysis and at the same time as the page reads of the check above.

- **What it reads.** The system prompt (`buildVerifierSystemPrompt`) names today's date and the lookback of every category; the user prompt (`buildVerifierUserPrompt`) holds the vendor and, for each finding, its claim, its impact and, for each source it cites, the address, the date, the title, the quote and, for an entry of a public database that code looked up for the vendor, the name of that database (the `database` attribute: "CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities Catalog", "National Vulnerability Database", "CVE Program", "Have I Been Pwned", "SEC EDGAR", "the vendor's status page" or the name of a list of the vendor risk database, a sanctions list, an AI list or one of the 23 lists of 2026-11-10 among them; `VERIFIER_DATABASE_NAMES` in `prompts.ts`, 39 names in all). It holds no other web text. For every finding the model answers three questions with true or false: `same_company` (are the sources about the vendor and not about another company with a similar name?), `quote_supports_claim` (does the quote, read on its own, say what the claim says? A source with a database is an entry of that public database, and that it is an entry of that database is part of what it says: an entry of the CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities Catalog says that the flaw is exploited in the wild, while an entry of the National Vulnerability Database or of the CVE Program says only that the flaw was published) and `source_in_window` (is the matter inside the lookback of its category?). It is told to answer true when it cannot tell, because a finding that is dropped by mistake is lost.
- **A false answer drops the finding.** It is not stored. The [trace](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-run-trace) counts it in `dropped` (`verifier.other_company`, `verifier.not_supported`, `verifier.out_of_window`; a finding that fails several questions is counted once, under the first in that order) and keeps a rejection (`stage: "verifier"`) with the start of the claim. A category whose candidates were all taken away ends like one whose candidates were already known: "Possible risks were found but could not be verified or are already recorded".
- **Only an answer takes a finding away.** A finding with no verdict is kept. So is every finding when the call does not succeed (30 seconds, retries included; an error; an answer that does not fit the schema): the call is recorded as failed (`purpose: "verifier"`, `ok: false`, the tokens it used) without the message of its error, which can quote the request or hold a key. The verifier never fails a run.
- **Off unless asked for.** `analyse` and `runVendorResearch` run it only with `verifier: true`, as `gather` and `perCategory` are on only when asked for. The job and live evaluations turn it on. A case of the evaluation can replay it (`verifier` in the case and in the recording; the case `verifier-drops-findings` holds a finding about another company, one whose quote says less than its claim and one that is too old).

### Credibility

Every finding that stays gets a credibility label, `low`, `medium` or `high`, computed by code from the sources it cites (`credibilityOf` in `credibility.ts`, called by `analyse`); every run stores it and the screen shows it in place of the confidence that earlier runs showed. No model sees, writes or decides it. It says how well the sources establish the facts, not how likely or how serious the risk is. The design is in sections D3 (the registry) and D5 (the rule) of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-vendor-risk-database-design.md`.

- **The source registry.** `sourceRegistry/entries.ts` is version `sr-6` (`REGISTRY_VERSION`): 119 entries, 25 official (governments, regulators, courts, official databases), 78 trusted press and 16 unverified. A source falls in one of four categories: `official`, `trusted_press`, `vendor` (the vendor speaking for itself) and `unverified` (everything else, a host the registry does not know included). An entry names its hosts, or a suffix, with the reason, the evidence, the day it was reviewed and the paths that downgrade it (`DowngradeRule`: a segment, the first segments, the first segments with `#` for digits, a slug, or a host); there is no entry that matches by a pattern. sr-2 added the answers of Q25 and Q26: the forums and communities of G11 and the platforms where users publish (G6–G9) are user content, and the documents that others file with an authority are unverified on an official entry (EDGAR filings under `/Archives`, public comments on regulations.gov, RECAP documents on CourtListener); sr-3 added their other addresses (EDGAR's viewers `/ix`, `/ixviewer` and `/cgi-bin/viewer`, the download host `downloads.regulations.gov`, CourtListener's document pages `/docket/<id>/<number>/…`); sr-4 (M2) made an EDGAR filing under the CIK a person stored for the vendor the vendor's own word, in the archive and in the viewers. `sr-5` is one version for four decisions of 2026-10-31 and 2026-11-01. **Q8, government domains**: the entry that matched any `gov`, `govt`, `gouv`, `gob` or `gv` label before a country code by a regular expression is gone, since 87% of the 1,240 names it matched were ordinary registrations, and a government domain is official only when a page of its registry, its regulator or its holder was read. `.mil`, gov.br, go.id, go.th, go.tz, gov.sg, gov.my, gov.pk, gob.pk, gov.lk, gov.za, gov.pl, gov.hk, gob.gt, gc.ca and gov.de are official entries with the page that supports each read; bund.de, gov.fi and gov.co are official entries whose operators' own pages were read but do not state one of the criteria (evidence status P); govt.nz, go.ke, go.ug, gov.ug and gov.ie have no entry, so they are unverified until a person reads their rule. **Q9, trusted press, under a ruling on evidence**: a page counts as evidence only when its site's `robots.txt` does not bar Anthropic's agents. The entries of the BBC, The Guardian, USA Today, CNBC, the Sydney Morning Herald, Bloomberg, The Washington Post, Forbes, CBC, Dark Reading and SC Media took the reason, the evidence and the paths that downgrade sponsored, advisory or council content that Q9 gives them (the Guardian's evidence is its editorial code alone, since theguardian.com bars Claude-User); The Hacker News is unverified, since no standards or corrections page of it was seen; the other publishers stay as sr-4 had them. **Rule V** was widened (below). **Q28** is in the classifier (below). `sr-6` (2026-11-05) adds six entries for the hosts of the AI databases (Q30), reviewed on that day with evidence status P: `incidentdatabase.ai` (`ai-incident-database`), `aiaaic.org` (`aiaaic`), `oecd.ai` (`oecd-ai`), `avidml.org` (`avid`) and `airisk.mit.edu` (`mit-ai-incident-tracker`) are unverified, each with its reason, and `atlas.mitre.org` (`mitre-atlas`, publisher MITRE ATLAS) is trusted press, because MITRE curates the ATLAS case studies from published research and incident reports with references for each. No data is copied for the five unverified hosts: a host the registry does not know is unverified and unreviewed already, so their entries only replace the mark "unreviewed" on a page that a web search cites with a reason. Each entry names the host of the database and not its owner's (`mitre.org`, `mit.edu` and `oecd.org` stay unknown), and the `oecd.ai` entry covers every page of its host, the OECD's own policy pages included, so it has to be revisited if a finding ever needs them. There is no entry for GW DAIL (a blog under `gwu.edu`: an entry would label the whole university) or for the Agent Incident Registry (its host was not verified), and `nhtsa.gov` and `fda.gov` are official already under the `.gov` entry. The six entries have no named review owner yet (Q18 of the spec is open); the test of the registry lists them as due from 2027-11-05 and fails on them from 2028-05-05. Every change is a pull request with a reason, evidence, a review date and a new version; the test of the registry names an entry reviewed 12 months ago and fails on one reviewed 18 months ago.
- **The classifier** (`sourceRegistry/classify.ts`) decides the category of a cited address in this order: user content is `unverified` (Q25: a forum or community section or host of G11 on any site, the vendor's own included, and a platform of G6–G9 unless the page is on the vendor's own subdomain of it, such as `acme.substack.com`); the vendor's own site is `vendor`; the host is normalised; the longest host entry is taken, then the longest suffix; a blocked entry; a downgrade (the paths of the entry, and the opinion, paid and press-release segments and the `sponsored.`, `paidpost.` and `partners.` hosts of any trusted publisher); a syndicated copy, credited to the publisher that its canonical link names (one level deep, never the vendor, an unknown host or another copy) or to its issuer; a paid wire, `vendor` when its text carries an e-mail address on the vendor's domain or a subdomain of it (Q27, `emailOnDomain`) and `unverified` otherwise; else the category of the entry. A filing in the EDGAR archive or viewer under the CIK stored for the vendor is `vendor` only when the filing's header names that CIK as a filer, a person it is filed by or a reporting owner (Q28, `edgarFiler.ts`: `filerRoleOf` answers `vendor`, `other` or no answer); with `other` or no answer it falls to the Q26 downgrade, `unverified`, for the archive path and the viewer forms alike. A record of a public database is classified by its database (`classifyRecord`, `recordMatchOf`; a record of the connector's SEC read-through keeps its strength, which does not depend on who filed): the catalog of exploited vulnerabilities and the NVD are official with a strong match (they name the vendor's product), the SEC and the three sanctions lists official with a weak one (found by the name alone), the Consolidated Screening List official with a weak one too (an entry of an OFAC list that carries the vendor's verified website or its LEI is strong), NHTSA's crash reports, the FDA list of AI-enabled devices and the FDA recalls of those devices official with a weak one too, MITRE ATLAS trusted press with a weak one (MITRE curates the case studies with references, but it is not an authority, and the match is by name), the AI Incident Database unverified with a weak one (an aggregator of allegations), Have I Been Pwned unverified, and the status page is the vendor's. The 23 lists of 2026-11-10 are classified the same way (`RECORD_CLASSES`; the registry stays `sr-6`): sixteen are official with a weak match (Canada's sanctions list, the California breach list, the UK OFSI, CMA, MHRA and ATRS lists, the FTC penalty and nonmerger lists, the EU competition cases, the Colombian sanctions, the Canadian and US federal AI registers, the Dutch register, NHTSA's recalls and investigations and the FedRAMP Marketplace), four are trusted press with a weak one (the AI Hallucination Cases database, the Foundation Model Transparency Index, Epoch AI's data and the C2PA conforming products list), two are unverified with a weak one (the PipeRoll registry, which one editor keeps, and the disclose.io list of community contributions), and GLEIF is trusted press with a strong one, since it is matched by the LEI. A record of the vendor risk database carries the strength of its own match instead, so a UK entry found by the vendor's verified website is strong.
- **Rule V.** A source of the vendor is a statement when the finding is in an event category (`security_incidents`, `availability`, `legal_regulatory`, `financial_health`), when it is the status page, or when it is a page of the vendor's site whose first section, after a locale (and after `intl` and a locale, as in `/intl/en-us/legal`) or after one of the parent words `company`, `about`, `about-us` or `corporate`, is a trust, security, legal, privacy, terms, policy, compliance, subprocessor or status section (the run-together names `privacypolicy`, `termsofservice`, `termsofuse` and `website-terms` among them), or whose host begins with `trust.`, `security.` or `status.` (`isStatementPage`, versioned with the registry). A page with a blog, news, newsroom, press or media section never is, at any depth, and the title of a page is not read. Anywhere else it is a self-claim, which never raises a label.
- **The rule.** A finding whose quote the snippet check did not find on the page is `low` (`contradicted`). Of the other sources, a record that its database withdrew, rejected or delisted supports nothing (the vendor risk database offers a run only the records its lists still hold, so a cited record leaves `active` only later, by the status job, see [Vendor risk database](#vendor-risk-database)), and a record found by name only supports `low` at most. A record of a list that gives context only (`CONTEXT_ONLY_RECORD_SOURCES`, ten lists: see **The third round of lists** above) supports nothing either: it is left out of the supporting sources before any rule is applied, so that a strong FedRAMP record beside a KEV record leaves the label to the KEV record, a strong GLEIF record is no trusted publisher, and a finding that cites nothing but such records gets the last rule, `low` by `unverified`, withdrawn or not (the rule `inactive` looks at the other sources only); the note `name_only` is still set from every cited record, so a weak record of such a list beside a strong KEV record leaves the label to the KEV record and keeps the note. The label is then `high` by the first of `official_record` (an official record matched strongly), `official_page`, `vendor_statement` and `trusted_press_two` (trusted press of two registry entries; one entry is one publisher) that holds with a source that was read. When one of them holds only with search results that nobody could confirm on a page, the label is capped at `medium` (`capped_unconfirmed`). Then `medium` for trusted press of exactly one publisher (`trusted_press_one`), counting the strong sources only: a weak record is no publisher for this rule either, so a case study of MITRE ATLAS, whose class is trusted press but whose match is always by name, never makes a label `medium`, and a finding whose only trusted-press source is such a record is `low` (`name_only`). Otherwise the label is `low` (`name_only`, `unverified`, `self_claim`, and `inactive` for a cited record that its list no longer stands by, a record of a list that gives context only apart). The note `name_only` ("this may be another company") is set whenever a cited record matched by name only, whatever the label.
- **The basis.** With the label comes the reason for it (`CredibilityBasis` in `i.vendorResearch.ts`): the rule, the version of the registry, the note, and each cited source as the rule saw it (its category, publisher, registry entry and reading, the vendor's kind of word, the database, match and status of a record and, for a record of the vendor risk database, its id in the list, the day of the copy and whether the copy was stale, or, once the status job changed its status, the day of the change and the list that rejected its CVE when that is another, `rejected_by`, and the downgraded, syndicated and blocked marks).
- **There is one set of rules.** The vendor risk database has no flag, so every run is rated by the credibility rules, and the earlier path of the model's confidence is gone: the analysis asks with `CREDIBILITY_PROMPT_VERSION` (`vr-15`), whose prompt leaves out the confidence field and its rule, words the databases (the CVE Program beside the NVD, most of whose flaws are not exploited) and the six sanctions and screening lists, the UN's, Canada's and the US Consolidated Screening List's among them (an entry matched by name only is written "An entity named … is on …", and one matched by the vendor's verified website or one of its identifiers, a registration number or an LEI, is about the vendor unless the identifiers it lists say otherwise, and a designation in force is a risk in legal and regulatory matters; a listing on the Entity List or the Military End User List is an export licence requirement and not a sanction), words the AI databases (an entry of them is matched by name only and is written "An entity named … appears in …", with at most one risk for each of the AI Incident Database, MITRE ATLAS, NHTSA and the FDA recalls, in AI-specific risks, a likelihood of Possible at most and an action plan that checks who the vendor is; an entry of the FDA list raises no risk; a recall is not a finding that the AI failed, and the recalling firm may be another company than the vendor), words the lists of 2026-11-10 by their class (an entry of an AI incident, enforcement and case, product safety or data breach list raises at most one risk, and an entry of a register or status list none), and whose schema has no confidence field. The research reads the news in the order of the registry's categories, and a source of a blocked registry entry is dropped from the evidence pack before the analysis (`buildEvidencePack(entries, { dropBlocked })`, counted as `source.blocked` in the trace). Before the labels are worked out, the analysis asks who filed each EDGAR filing that a kept finding cites under the vendor's stored CIK (Q28; at most 5 headers a run, with the SEC User-Agent and a turn of the SEC spacer; see [web-research](../infrastructure/web-research.md#records-of-public-databases)). A run from before the flag was removed may hold the model's confidence and no label; the reads give it its confidence as its label until the backfill.
- **Stored.** `withCredibility` (`pipeline.ts`) gives each kept finding `credibility` and `credibility_basis`, and each of its sources the category, publisher, flags and record (`source_id`, `record_id`, `status`, `match_strength`) that the basis gives it; the columns are in `20261030223852-vendor-finding-credibility.js`, which also lets `confidence` be null for the milestone that drops it.
- **The line.** The rationale of a label is one line written by code from the basis, never by a model (`credibilityRationale` in `credibilityText.ts`): the sentence of the rule that decided it ("High: CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities lists it (CVE-…), as of 7 Oct 2026.", one sentence for each of the eleven rules), then, when a cited copy of the vendor risk database was stale, "The source has not been updated since {date}.", then the name-only note, "Matched by name only: this may be another company.", when the rule is not already `name_only`. A record of a list that gives context only never shapes the line: a finding that cites nothing else reads "Low: only registers that list the vendor are cited, and a register entry is not evidence of a risk.", the stale sentence and the sentence of a withdrawn record leave such records out, and the name-only note counts them like any other. The API adds this English line to every finding (`credibility_rationale`); the client words the same line from the basis in the person's language (see [Vendor research](#vendor-research)).
- **Evaluation.** Each expected finding of the replay cases names the label it should get, every case holds `maxMislabelled: 0`, eight cases are worked cases of the rule, nine more replay copies of the lists through the lookup's own `offerRecords` (a withdrawn CVE, a delisted sanctions entry, a stale copy, one name on two lists, an entry whose website is a page on the platform that holds the vendor's own page, `platform-website`, a KEV entry whose CVE the CVE Program rejected, offered by no list, `rejected-cve-mirror`, a UK entry with the vendor's name and registration number, High, `identifier-match`, a CVE that the NVD answered at run time, High as of the day of the answer, `nvd-read-through`, and an EU entry that carries the vendor's LEI under another name, High, `lei-match-other-name`), (see [Evaluating the agent](../infrastructure/web-research.md#evaluating-the-agent); the replay answers the one set of rules, and the scorer has no `overconfident` measure any more).

### Backfill of the label (M3)

A finding of a run from before the credibility rules has a confidence and no label (`credibility` is null). Until the backfill gives it one, the reads show that confidence as its label: a finding with a confidence and no label reads as that level on the basis `{ "rule": "legacy_confidence" }` (`findingColumn` in `utils/vendorResearch.utils.ts`: `COALESCE(credibility, confidence)`), and the API words it "Rated before the source rules; this is the earlier confidence level until the label is recomputed." (`credibility_rationale`; the client words it in the person's language). The backfill gives such a finding its own label, once for each deployment, before the follow-up release drops the `confidence` column (Part B2 of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-31-vendor-risk-database-m3.md`, on `feature/vendor-finding-drop-confidence`). Nothing runs it at startup or in a migration: a maintainer runs `Servers/scripts/vendorResearchBackfillCredibility.ts`. The decision is `services/vendorResearch/backfill.ts` (pure), the queries are `utils/vendorResearchBackfill.utils.ts`.

- **A pending finding** is decided by the rule's own parts (`describeSources` and `decideCredibility` in `credibility.ts`), from what its run stored: the run's `evidence` (the pack its analysis read), the run's `trace`, and the vendor as it is now (`buildTarget`). A cited address that the stored pack lacks reads as unconfirmed, so a run that kept no evidence gives at most Medium (`capped_unconfirmed`). The label is stored with the basis under the rule's backfill name, `backfill_<rule>` (`backfill_official_record`, `BackfilledCredibilityBasis`), and the line is the rule's sentence followed by "Rated again with the current rule." The backfill writes the label and its basis only, so the finding's `sources` stay as its run stored them, without categories; the screen takes each source's badge from the basis, from the source at the same place with the same address (`categoryOf` in `FindingRow.tsx`).
- **An accepted or dismissed finding** keeps the confidence the person decided it by: `credibility = confidence` and the basis `{ "rule": "legacy_confidence" }` (`LegacyCredibilityBasis`); the line is the legacy sentence above. No screen shows that line for a decided finding today: the Risks tab lists pending findings only, and the API returns decided ones with `status=accepted` or `status=dismissed`. A decided finding without a confidence is left as it is. `StoredCredibilityBasis` (the rule's basis, a backfilled one or a legacy one) types the column on the server and in the client, which words both notes in German, French and Spanish too.
- **The records by their addresses.** The per-run lookups kept no provenance, so the addresses they cited stand for it (`provenanceByAddress`, only these exact `https` forms, at most 2,048 characters): `https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/<CVE>` is an NVD record, strong (the per-run KEV records cited the same page); `https://sanctionssearch.ofac.treas.gov/Details.aspx?id=<n>` an OFAC entry, weak, with the name-only note; `https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/<cik>/<accession>/<document>`, whose `<accession>` folder has 18 digits, an SEC filing, strong only when the CIK stored on the vendor is `<cik>` as a number, weak otherwise; `https://haveibeenpwned.com/api/v3/breach/<name>` a Have I Been Pwned breach, with no match; `https://<host>/history` and `https://<host>/incidents/<id>` a status page record, the vendor's statement, when `<host>` is one the status page lookup asks for the vendor's domain (`statusHostsOf`): its own status host (`status.<domain>`) whoever read the page, and the brand's hosts (`<brand>.statuspage.io`, `<brand>status.com`), which could be another company's, only when the stored pack holds the address. A lookup put its records into the ledger before the research, as pages under its database's category (`RECORD_CATEGORY`: `security_incidents` for the NVD, the SEC and Have I Been Pwned, `legal_regulatory` for OFAC, `availability` for the status page), and the ledger holds an address once: when the stored pack holds the address as anything else, under another category or as a search result, the research found the page itself, and it is read as any page, as at run time (an EDGAR filing found under certifications is read as any page; the backfill makes no request, so no filer of a filing under the stored CIK is known and it reads as `unverified`, as the rule of Q28 says). Each such record is `active`, its id is the CVE, the OFAC id, the accession number with its dashes (`0000320193-26-000001`, as the SEC writes it and the live SEC records carry it) or the breach name (a status page record has none, as at run time), and its `as_of` is the day of the finding (UTC), the day the lookup read it.
- **The verdict of the snippet check**, from the run's trace first (`verdictOf`): the steps `by: "verification"`, `tool: "read"` whose address is one the finding cites and whose category is the finding's or absent. A finding that cites a page the run read (the stored pack holds the address as `kind: "page"`) has no verdict, as at run time, where the check examines no such finding: a step of one of its addresses was another finding's, since the trace keeps no finding id. For a snippet-only finding, a check that found the quote off the page makes it `contradicted`; any other check makes it unread; with no check in the trace, the spec's rule decides (a stored `low` was contradicted at its first cited address, any other was unread). For another finding, a check that confirmed the quote makes that source read.
- **The status job** never decides a backfilled or legacy label again (`relabelFinding` skips a rule that is not one of the eleven): its sources have no record of the vendor risk database to follow.
- **The script.** The default is a dry run, which writes nothing. `--apply` writes, with no flag to turn on: every run is rated by the credibility rules, and the reads already show the old confidence as a legacy label. `--undo` clears what the backfill wrote (a rule `legacy_confidence` or one that starts with `backfill_`), but for the `backfill_` label of a finding a person accepted or dismissed since: that label is what they decided the finding by (D4.6), and a later `--apply` would give the finding its old confidence instead, so it is kept and counted ("organization 1: cleared 3, kept 1 (decided after the backfill)"). `--organization N` limits the work to one organization (exit 2 for one that does not exist). It walks the organizations in id order, one at a time, and reads the findings without a label of one organization in batches of 500, each after the last id; every statement but the list of the organizations filters on `organization_id`. The write is guarded by `status = :status AND credibility IS NULL`, so a finding a person decided, or a live run labelled, since the read is skipped and counted; a later run of the script gives one decided meanwhile its carried-over label. It prints organization ids and counts only ("organization 1: 3 pending (high 1, medium 0, low 2), 2 decided (legacy 2)", "total: 3 pending, 2 decided; dry run, nothing written", "written 4, skipped 1 (decided or labelled meanwhile)"), and an error by its name. Exit codes: 0 done, 1 the database failed, 2 usage or refusal.

Run it where the servers run. The backend image has no `npx` (`Servers/Dockerfile` removes npm and npx), so a deployment from the image runs the compiled script, `dist/scripts/vendorResearchBackfillCredibility.js`, in the backend's container (`docker compose exec backend …`, or `kubectl exec -n <namespace> deploy/backend -- …` with the Helm chart, whose deployment is named `backend`). From a checkout, `npx ts-node` runs the source.

```bash
docker compose exec backend node dist/scripts/vendorResearchBackfillCredibility.js            # dry run
docker compose exec backend node dist/scripts/vendorResearchBackfillCredibility.js --apply
docker compose exec backend node dist/scripts/vendorResearchBackfillCredibility.js --undo
cd Servers && npx ts-node scripts/vendorResearchBackfillCredibility.ts                         # from a checkout
```

### Limits

| Limit | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Steps of the research loop | 24, invalid ones included |
| `search` actions per run | 8, each with a 12 second and 1 MB limit; one at a time per process, at least a second apart |
| `read` actions per run | 12, successful or not; a page takes at most 20 seconds, 2 MB after decompression and 4 redirects |
| One model turn of the loop, the whole loop | 60 seconds, 5 minutes |
| Snippet, page excerpt | 1,500 characters, 8,000 characters |
| PDF: body, pages read, text, time, heap of the thread, files at once | 10,485,760 bytes, 100 pages, 320,000 characters, 20,000 ms, 256 MiB, 2 |
| Page built by a script, read in the headless browser (off unless `VENDOR_RESEARCH_HEADLESS` is `true`): requests, bytes, time, pages at once | 60, 12 MiB, 20,000 ms, 2 |
| Parts of a long page, parts kept as sources, text of a page source | 40 parts of at most 8,000 characters, 2 parts, 24,010 characters |
| Evidence pack | 100,000 bytes serialized; trimmed from the largest category, the records of the registers first, see [Research](#research) |
| Pages where the vendor states its terms | 8 documents for a run; a kept page is at most 4,000 lines and 100,000 characters (a line is cut at 600); forgotten after 365 days |
| Source of the change of such a page | 6,000 characters, a line at most 400 |
| Model call of the analysis | 120 seconds, retries included |
| Lookup in the public databases (the CISA catalog, the NVD, Have I Been Pwned, the SEC), on the status page of the vendor and in the sanctions list | 8 seconds for the six together; at most 5, 3, 3, 3, 4 (a summary and 3 incidents for the status page) and 2 records; paid for by no budget |
| Reads to check the findings that rest on snippets | 2 pages for a finding, one deadline of 20 seconds for all the reads |
| Records of the vendor risk database offered to a run | 5 of the catalog; 3 of each of the NVD, the SEC, the CVE records, MITRE ATLAS, the NHTSA crash reports, the FDA recalls and the AI Incident Database; 2 of each sanctions list, of the Consolidated Screening List and of the FDA list; of the 23 lists of 2026-11-10, 2 of ten of them (`canada_sema`, `california_breaches`, `charlotin`, `ftc_penalties`, `eu_dg_comp`, `ftc_competition`, `co_sic_penalties`, `nhtsa_recalls`, `nhtsa_odi`, `fedramp`) and 1 of each of the other thirteen; a copy is stale 48 hours after its last success (192 hours for a list copied once a week) |
| Model call of the verifier | 30 seconds, retries included |
| Findings kept | 3 per category, 8 per run |
| Manual cooldown | `VENDOR_RESEARCH_MANUAL_COOLDOWN_MINUTES` (10), per vendor |
| Scheduled scan | Once an hour, at minute 11 (UTC); for each organization that chose an interval, at most 5 vendors and only while the runs of the last 24 hours are under half of the daily cap |
| Daily cap | `VENDOR_RESEARCH_DAILY_RUN_CAP` (50), per organization, rolling 24 hours, automatic and manual runs together |
| A stuck queued run | 15 minutes (`STALE_RUN_MINUTES`) |
| A run's own time limit | 20 minutes (`RUN_TIME_LIMIT_MINUTES`); the sweep fails a running run after 25 (`STALE_RUNNING_RUN_MINUTES`) |

- The cooldown applies to manual starts only, but it looks at the vendor's newest run of any trigger type, so an automatic run holds off a manual one. An automatic start does not wait for the cooldown; it is held to the daily cap. The same holds for a scheduled start, and the scan stops at half of the cap (see [The scheduled scan](#the-scheduled-scan)).
- Both limits are plain count queries over `vendor_research_runs`. They count every run except two kinds that did no research and called no model, so they count for neither:
  - a run that **failed before it started** (its job could not be queued, or it was swept as stuck while still `queued`). A start refused with 503 can therefore be tried again at once;
  - a run that is **still `queued` more than 15 minutes after it was created** (`STALE_RUN_MINUTES`, the limit of the stuck-run sweep below). Its job was lost, for example because Redis was emptied. (The backend empties the queue of the automations each time it starts, `obliterate` in `scheduleVendorReviewDateNotification`, which used to take the waiting research jobs with it; the research queue is never emptied.) The sweep only looks at the vendor that is started or whose latest run is read, so without this rule such a run could use up a slot of the daily cap for the whole 24 hours. A worker that picks up the job of such a run later does not start it either (the start has the same 15-minute limit), so the run can only end as `timed out`, without a web request or a model call.

  A `running` run counts however long it has run, and so do a completed run and a run that failed after it started.
- A run that has been `queued` for more than 15 minutes, or `running` for more than 25 (counted from `started_at`, or from `created_at` if it never started), is failed with the error `timed out`. The 25 minutes are the run's own limit plus 5 minutes of grace: the pipeline fails a run that runs past `RUN_TIME_LIMIT_MINUTES` (20) itself, so the sweep only reaches a running run whose worker died, never one that is still working. The end of a run is guarded: the finish (`finishRunQuery`) writes only a run that is `queued` or `running`, so a run that the sweep failed is never overwritten by a late worker, and a run that ended while the pipeline was saving its findings loses those findings (the transaction rolls back) rather than the end. There is no background sweep: it runs before a new run is created and whenever the latest run is read. So a worker that is down leaves a run `queued` until the next start or read, and neither a crash nor a restart blocks a vendor or leaves the UI on "Researching" for ever.
- A queued run also waits for a free place on the research worker. Research has a queue of its own, `vendor-research`, and a worker of its own (`services/vendorResearch/queue.ts` and `worker.ts`), started with the other workers by `npm run worker` (`jobs/worker.ts`). The worker takes the runs of that queue only, `VENDOR_RESEARCH_WORKER_CONCURRENCY` at a time for each worker process (5 unless set, see [Configuration](#configuration)), and a run can hold a place for up to about 7 minutes (5 for the research loop, 2 for the analysis, the checks after it half a minute). Runs and the other background jobs (notifications, recomputes, reports) no longer wait for each other. A burst of runs can still leave the last one queued past the 15-minute limit, which the sweep ends as `timed out`; this takes many runs started at once (for example many vendors created in a short time, each with an automatic run) or a worker that is down. The vendor then shows the run as `timed out`, which counts toward neither limit, and **Research again** starts a new one at once. The remedy for a burst is a higher setting or another worker process.
- Each run costs at most 24 steps of the loop (each step is one structured call, which can be retried when the answer does not fit), the analysis call (more only when an answer has to be asked for again), one call of the verifier over the findings that stay, 8 searches and 12 page reads, and at most two more page reads for each finding that rests on snippets. A typical run is 8 to 16 steps, roughly 60,000 to 130,000 input tokens for the loop and 20,000 to 30,000 for the analysis: cents on a DeepSeek-class price, a dollar or more on a frontier model. These are estimates. The organization pays on its own key, and the daily cap bounds it. `searches_used` and `pages_read` measure the tool side; the provider's usage page measures the model side.

### Accept and dismiss

`acceptFinding` and `dismissFinding` are in `services/vendorResearch/findings.service.ts`.

**Accept** checks the edits of the request (see [Vendor Research Endpoints](#vendor-research-endpoints)) and then is one transaction. A bad edit is answered 400 before anything is locked, read or written.

1. The finding row is locked with `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` (organization and id). A lock that is not available means another accept, or the status job relabelling the finding, holds it at this moment, and it is answered 409 at once with "This finding is being updated. Try again in a moment." (the finding may still be pending, so it is not said to be decided): a request that waits holds a pooled connection while the lock holder still needs more, and accepts that waited could use up the pool between them. A retry can succeed if the other decision let go without deciding.
2. A missing finding, or one whose vendor is gone, is a 404. A finding that is not `pending` is a 409.
3. The action owner is resolved (below).
4. The vendor risk is created with `createVendorRiskInTransaction` (`services/vendors/vendorRiskCreate.service.ts`): the insert into `vendorrisks` with the description, impact, likelihood, severity, action plan and risk level (each the edit of the request when there is one, else the finding's; no order number), the frameworks the person picked (none are passed when none were, because a failed framework insert aborts the whole transaction and so the accept), and the change-history record of the creation by the user. The vendor risk form (`createVendorRisk`) calls the same function, so a risk created either way is created by the same rules. The vendor is the finding's own, whatever the request says.
5. The finding is marked `accepted`, with `accepted_vendor_risk_id`, `decided_by` and `decided_at`.

Anything that goes wrong rolls the whole accept back and leaves the finding `pending`. Only after the commit does `runVendorRiskPostCommitEffects` run, again the same as for the form: the related-risk recompute job, the candidate notice to the owners of project risks in the vendor's use cases, and the action owner's assignment notice, each fire-and-forget. A rolled-back accept announces nothing.

**Edits and atomicity.** The risk the person saw and edited is created and the finding is settled in the same transaction, so a risk is never created without its finding being accepted, nor a finding accepted without its risk. If anything fails (a framework that does not exist, a lock held by another decision), the finding stays `pending` and the form that sent the request stays open with what was typed.

**Default action owner.** With no `action_owner` in the request, the risk's owner is the vendor's assignee, but only if that person is a user of the organization; otherwise the risk has no owner. Nothing in the database ties an assignee to the vendor's organization, and an owner from another organization would be sent a notice with the risk's text and the vendor's name. The accept goes on without an owner and a log line with ids says why. `action_owner: null` means nobody, and a number must be a user of the organization.

**Dismiss** is one conditional update (`WHERE status = 'pending'`), so a finding is dismissed once and never after it was accepted. The reason is one of `not_applicable`, `already_covered`, `inaccurate`, `accepted_risk`, `wrong_vendor`, `outdated` and `other`. `wrong_vendor` and `outdated` are the two that blame the research and not the risk: the sources were about another company, or too old. Every decision is also counted by a metric (see [Decision statistics](#vendor-research-endpoints)). The note is optional: `null`, an empty string and blanks all mean no note, and a note is cleaned of control characters and trimmed before it is counted against the 500-character limit. The finding keeps its wording, which is what stops similar candidates from coming back (see [De-duplication](#de-duplication)).

### Notice to the assignee

After an **automatic** or a **scheduled** run that stored at least one finding, the vendor's assignee (when the vendor has one, and that person is a user of the vendor's organization) gets a `vendor_research_findings` in-app notification that links to `/vendors/risks?findingsVendorId=<vendor id>`. The organization check is the one the accept uses for the default action owner (`findUsersNotInOrganization`): vendor writes do not check the assignee, so it can be a user of another organization, and for such a person no notification is written, nothing is raised, the run is unaffected, and one debug line with ids only is logged. A manual run does not notify: the person who started it is looking at the result. A scheduled run notifies for the same reason an automatic one does: nobody started it, so nobody is looking. The notification type, link, metadata and dedup index are described in [Notifications](./notifications.md#vendor-research-notice).

### Safety

- **Web text is untrusted.** It enters the prompts inside a delimited block with its markup characters escaped. The model of the loop can only name a search or an address, and code decides what happens (next bullet); the analysis has no actions, its answer is bound to a schema and then checked by code against the retrieved text, and nothing is written to the register unless a person accepts. The findings section says they are AI-suggested and must be verified, and the UI shows finding text, quotes and model notes as plain text, never as markup.
- **Email.** The text of an accepted finding reaches the action owner's assignment email like any risk text. That email HTML-escapes the names it shows (the mail compiler escapes every plain value) and the context it shows (the service escapes each value with `escapeHtml` of `utils/sanitization.utils.ts`; see [Notifications](./notifications.md#assignment-emails)), so text that a model wrote after reading a web page shows as typed and cannot add a tag, a link or an image.
- **Links.** A source is a link only when its URL is `http` or `https`; it opens in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`. The server drops any other URL from the evidence before the model sees it.
- **The server fetches pages the model chose, behind a guard.** That is a server-side request forgery surface, so every request goes through `guardedFetch` of the [web research toolkit](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-page-guard). Only `http` and `https` on ports 80 and 443 are used, and never an IP address, a URL with credentials, `localhost` or a name that is reserved for private networks. Every address the host resolves to must be public, checked on the connection itself, so DNS cannot answer differently afterwards. Redirects are followed by hand (4 at most) and each hop is checked again, and a redirect from https to http is refused. A page may be read only if a search of the run returned its address or it is on the vendor's domain, so a page cannot send the agent to an arbitrary address. The body is cut at 2 MB after decompression, and the whole fetch at 20 seconds. The bot says who it is (`VerifyWiseResearchBot`), sends no cookie, credential or `Referer`, and obeys each site's `robots.txt` for pages.
- **No search key.** The search needs no account and no key: an Anthropic key searches with Claude on the key it has, and every other key with the GDELT Project, which needs none. The LLM key is the organization's own: it is never returned (the config endpoint returns booleans) and never logged. The pipeline logs ids only (organization, vendor and run), never page text, URLs, queries, model output or keys, and the errors of the page guard and of the search are fixed phrases that repeat nothing of the URL or the query.
- **Stored errors are fixed phrases.** A run is served to every role, so its `error` is always one of: `Vendor not found`; `No LLM key configured`; `Model call failed: HTTP <status>`, `... timed out`, `... the answer did not match the expected format` or `... unexpected error` (from the loop or from the analysis); `The run stopped because the web could not be reached`; `The model did not use the research actions`; `Unexpected error`; `Saving the findings failed`; `timed out` (written by the sweep, and by the pipeline for a run that reaches its time limit); `could not enqueue`. The message of a model or provider error is neither stored nor logged by the pipeline (the log line has the error's name and HTTP status), and the message of any other error, which can name a host, a port or a query, goes to the server log only. The client maps this closed set to translated sentences and shows a generic one for anything else.
- **Category notes.** The note of a category is a fixed phrase from the set under [Category outcomes](#category-outcomes), except for a `nothing_found` category in which no search or page failed and the model proposed nothing: there it may be the model's own text (200 characters at most). The UI shows that one as plain text under "AI note (unverified)".
- **Data sent out.** During a run the server requests `api.gdeltproject.org` for a key that is not Anthropic's (one request: the vendor's names and the topic words of the news categories), searches through the provider of the research key for an Anthropic key (the queries the model writes: by its prompt the vendor's name and topic words, which the code does not enforce), and public pages, through the [page guard](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-page-guard): those on any host that a search returned, and those on the vendor's own domain and its subdomains, which its pages link to (a redirect can lead a request on to another public host, which the guard checks again). Every request names the bot. The LLM provider, which the organization chose, receives the vendor's name, its domain and what the vendor provides, the queries, the snippets and page text that the research read, and, in the analysis only, the vendor's scorecard answers on data sensitivity, business criticality and regulatory exposure. When an Admin chose another key for the analysis, each provider receives only its own part: the provider of the research key the loop's calls, the provider of the analysis key the vendor, the scorecard answers and the evidence. No other organization data leaves. The scheduled lists come from public hosts and nothing about any vendor goes to them; a read-through source is asked by one key of the vendor (GLEIF by its stored LEI, a public identifier, and by nothing else). An installation that cannot reach the web cannot use the feature fully: a run that finds no record in the public databases and whose calls the web never answers fails with "The run stopped because the web could not be reached" (a run that found records analyses them and completes).
- **Stored third-party text.** Page excerpts and snippets are kept in `vendor_research_runs.evidence` as an audit trail (up to 100 KB per run). The UI shows only the quotes (300 characters) and links. Pruning old evidence is out of scope.

### Permissions and multi-tenancy

- **Permissions.** Starting a run, accepting and dismissing need `vendor.edit` (`authorize("vendor.edit")`, the key of the vendor and vendor risk writes, so a role that cannot add a vendor risk by hand cannot add one by accepting a finding; Admin and Editor by default). The trace, the decision statistics and the settings need `llmKeys.admin`. Everything else, including the findings list, the latest run and the config, needs only a signed-in user, so every role can see what research found.
- **Tenant isolation.** Every query filters on `organization_id`. The organization and the user always come from the request, never from the body or the path. The `vendor_id` of a run or a finding is a foreign key to `vendors` only and is not tied to an organization in the database, so every id from a path is looked up in the caller's organization before it is used, and a vendor or finding of another organization is a 404, the same as one that does not exist. The job payload carries `organizationId`. The five tables are in the tenant-isolation registry with isolation tests, the settings of one organization cannot name the key of another (a 404), and the scan reads the vendors and the interval of one organization at a time.

### Configuration

| Variable | Default | Meaning |
|----------|---------|---------|
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_AUTO_RUN` | `true` | Research a vendor automatically after it is created. Only `false` (any case) turns it off; people can still start a run by hand |
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_MANUAL_COOLDOWN_MINUTES` | `10` | Minutes before the same vendor can be researched again by hand. `0` means no wait. Anything but a whole number of 0 or more falls back to 10 |
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_DAILY_RUN_CAP` | `50` | Runs one organization may start in a rolling 24 hours, automatic and manual together. Anything but a whole number of 1 or more falls back to 50 |
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_WORKER_CONCURRENCY` | `5` | Runs one worker process carries out at the same time. Anything but a whole number of 1 or more falls back to 5 |
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_HEADLESS` | `false` | Open a page that only a script can build in a headless Chromium, so that its text can be read (see [Rendering a page that a script builds](../infrastructure/web-research.md#rendering-a-page-that-a-script-builds)). Only `true` (any case) turns it on |
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS` | `true` | Look the vendor up in the [vendor risk database](#vendor-risk-database) (the scheduled copies of the lists, the read-through of the NVD, the SEC and GLEIF) and in the public databases that a run asks itself (Have I Been Pwned and the status page of the vendor; see [Records of public databases](#records-of-public-databases)). Only `false` (any case) turns it all off: no list is downloaded, nothing is looked up, and the coverage of a run says the databases are turned off. Both the backend and the worker read it |

The six variables are optional and are listed in `Servers/.env.example`. `VENDOR_RESEARCH_VRDB`, which turned the vendor risk database on while it was tested, is gone: the database is always on and nothing reads that variable, so a deployment that still sets it changes nothing. They are read from the process environment each time they are used, not at import. There is no search setting: research needs an LLM key saved for the organization, which an Admin adds in the LLM Keys section of Settings → API Keys, and nothing else. Without a key the feature reports "not set up" and nothing runs. The server also needs outbound internet access: HTTPS to `api.gdeltproject.org` (a key that is not Anthropic's searches the news there; an Anthropic key searches through its provider), to the public databases and their hosts (unless `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS` is `false`; the list is in the [web research toolkit](../infrastructure/web-research.md#what-leaves-the-server) and in `docs/deployment/CONFIGURATION_REFERENCE.md`) and to public websites (and HTTP on port 80 for the sites that only offer that).

**The scheduled scan has no variable.** It is a setting of the organization (**Research settings**), it runs on the same queue and worker as the runs, and the backend registers its hourly job at start (`jobs/producer.ts`). Its limits are the batch of 5 and half of `VENDOR_RESEARCH_DAILY_RUN_CAP`.

**The worker needs no search settings.** The backend reads the first three variables, for the config endpoint, the automatic start, the cooldown and the daily cap. The worker reads only `VENDOR_RESEARCH_WORKER_CONCURRENCY`, for the number of runs it carries out at once, `VENDOR_RESEARCH_HEADLESS`, for the browser that it opens a page built by a script in, and `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS`, for the lookup in the public databases: it takes the organization's LLM key from the database and runs the pipeline. It needs outbound internet access, though. A worker that cannot reach the web ends a run as `failed` with `The run stopped because the web could not be reached` when no call of the loop was answered and nothing is left to analyse; records of the public databases that the run found are analysed even when the web is down. `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS` is read by both processes: at start the backend registers the schedulers of the syncs while it is on and removes them otherwise (`scheduleVendorRiskDatabaseSync`), and the worker runs the syncs, checking it again, and looks the vendor up in the copies. Set it the same on the backend and on the worker.

The Kubernetes, Helm and Ansible templates do not set the vendor research variables, so the six take their defaults unless a value is added to the environment of the backend (the first three), of the worker (the next two) or of both (`VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS`).

| Deployment | What to do |
|------------|------------|
| `docker-compose.prod.yml` | Nothing beyond setting the variables in `.env.prod`: the file hands the whole `.env.prod` to the backend and the worker |
| `docker-compose.override.yml` (development stack) | It gives `.env.dev` to the backend but not to the worker. That is fine: only the backend reads the three variables, so every worker can run a job, whichever container it is in |
| `kubernetes/backend-deployment.yaml`, `kubernetes/worker-deployment.yaml` | These list variables by name and do not set the vendor research variables. Only the backend reads them, so a value other than the default has to be added to the backend deployment (or its config map) |
| Helm chart: `kubernetes/helm/verifywise/templates/backend-deployment.yaml`, `configmap.yaml`, `values.yaml` | Same: the chart does not set them. A value other than the default has to be added to the backend deployment, the config map and the values |
| Ansible: `ansible/templates/.env.j2` | Renders `.env.prod`, which compose hands to both processes. The template does not set them; add the lines for the values you want to change |

Network egress is part of the deployment. The research runs in the worker (in the development stack also in the backend container, which runs a worker of its own), and its requests are made directly from that process: the page guard reads no proxy setting and resolves the public host names itself. A firewall or network policy that blocks outbound HTTPS from the worker, or a network whose only way out is a proxy, makes every run that finds no record in the public databases fail with "The run stopped because the web could not be reached" (a run that found records analyses them and completes).

## Change History

All vendor changes are tracked:

| Field | Description |
|-------|-------------|
| entity_type | "vendor" or "vendor_risk" |
| entity_id | Vendor or risk ID |
| action | created/updated/deleted |
| changed_by | User ID |
| field_name | Changed field |
| old_value | Previous value |
| new_value | New value |
| timestamp | When changed |

## Frontend Structure

### Main Page

**Location:** `pages/Vendors/`

**Features:**
- Vendor list with search/filter/group
- Vendor risks tab
- Add/edit vendor modal
- Add/edit risk modal
- Export functionality
- Page tour for guidance

### Components

| Component | Purpose |
|-----------|---------|
| `AddNewVendor` | Create/edit vendor modal |
| `AddNewRisk` | Create/edit risk modal (Risk details, Custom fields, Activity, Linked risks); `initialTab="linked-risks"` opens an existing risk on its links. Given a `preview` (`VendorRiskPreview`) it is the **draft** of a research finding: pre-filled, vendor fixed, and its Save hands the form to `preview.onSave` instead of creating the risk itself (see [Vendor research](#vendor-research)) |
| `VendorRiskLinksPanel` | The Linked risks tab: child project risks and related vendor risks, plus Suggest children and the related scan |
| `RelateVendorRiskForm` | Picker that relates another vendor risk to this one |
| `InlineFormCard` | Bordered frame, title and Cancel/primary actions for the forms that open inside the Linked risks tab |
| `VendorRiskInsights` | Insights panel above the risks table: heat map, blast radius, duplicate and coverage tabs |
| `VendorResearchFindings` | Research findings section above the insights: pending findings with Preview and Dismiss, and the latest research run of a vendor (see [Vendor research](#vendor-research)) |
| `VendorResearchButton` | **Research risks** icon button in each row of the vendors table |
| `RiskTable` | Display vendor risks, including the Inherited by column |
| `TableWithPlaceholder` | Main vendor list |
| `GroupedTableView` | Grouped display |
| `FilterBy` | Dynamic filtering |

### Hooks

| Hook | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `useVendors()` | Fetch vendors |
| `useDeleteVendor()` | Delete mutation |
| `useVendorRisks()` | Fetch risks |
| `useDeleteVendorRisk()` | Delete risk mutation |
| `useVendorExposure()`, `useVendorDuplicateCandidates()`, `useVendorFrameworkCoverage()` | Vendor risk insight reports (`hooks/useRiskLinks.ts`) |
| `useSuggestVendorRiskHierarchy(id)` | Suggest children on one vendor risk |
| `useRecomputeVendorRiskLinks()` | Scan every vendor risk for related vendor risks (Admin) |
| `useVendorResearchConfig()` | Whether research is set up (`hooks/useVendorResearch.ts`, as are the rows below) |
| `useVendorRiskFindings(filter)` | The findings list (one status, one vendor or all) |
| `useLatestVendorResearch(vendorId)` | The latest run of a vendor, polled while it is queued or running, and read again on window focus while it is active. `restartPolling()` starts a new two-minute window and reads the run at once |
| `useStartVendorResearch()` | Start a run |
| `useAcceptFinding()`, `useDismissFinding()` | Decide a finding. Accept takes the body of `AcceptFindingBody`: the owner and the edits of the form |
| `followResearch(queryClient, vendorId)`, `useFollowedResearchVendor()` | Leave, and read once, the hint that makes the Risks tab follow a run started on the vendors table |

### Vendor research

**Findings section.** `pages/Vendors/VendorResearchFindings/` is mounted by `pages/Vendors/index.tsx` on the Risks tab (`/vendors/risks`), above the insights. It shows pending findings only: up to 200 at once, with a note when there are more, and accepted or dismissed findings are not listed.

- Collapsible like the Insights panel. It is open by default when something is pending, when a vendor comes from the URL, or when a run was followed from the vendors table (see **Following a run**), and the viewer's own toggle wins. The header says "Research findings", shows a "Pending: N" chip, and says "AI-suggested from web sources. Verify before accepting."
- A **Vendor** filter ("All vendors" or one vendor). It starts from the **`findingsVendorId`** URL parameter (digits only, above zero; anything else is ignored), and when the URL has none, from the vendor of a run followed from the vendors table (see **Following a run**); with neither it starts on all vendors. The parameter is the one that the assignee notification links to: `/vendors/risks?findingsVendorId=<vendor id>`. A new value in the URL while the page is mounted is followed. The existing `vendorId` parameter opens the vendor modal, so the section has a parameter of its own.
- Each row (`FindingRow`) shows the category, the vendor, the risk and impact text, the action plan under an "Action plan" caption (accepting writes it to the register, so the person who decides sees all three texts that will be written, and the form of **Preview** shows them again to change), chips for risk level, severity, likelihood and credibility (see below), the rationale under a "Why this rating" caption (plain text; nothing is shown for a finding without one, which is the case for the findings of runs from before the sentence existed), and the sources: the host as a link (`http` and `https` only), the publication date and the quote as plain text, under the line "Not confirmed on the page. The quotes come from search results." when the finding is `snippet_only`. The action plan is plain text like the other texts, and nothing is shown for a finding without one. The **Preview** and **Dismiss** buttons of a row are described by that row's risk text (`aria-describedby`), so a screen reader says which risk a button decides.
- **Preview** and **Dismiss** are hidden for users without `vendor.edit`, who see the section read-only. There is no one-click accept: a finding is added to the register through the vendor risk form, so the person sees, and can change, what will be written.
- **Preview** opens `AddNewRisk` (the vendor risk form) on the finding, in place on the Risks tab: nothing is requested and nothing is navigated to. The section passes a `VendorRiskPreview` (`domain/interfaces/i.vendor.ts`): a title ("Review suggested risk"), a description, the finding's `values` and an `onSave`. The form opens filled in with the finding's risk, impact, action plan, likelihood and severity (the register's top likelihood, `Almost certain`, is matched without regard to case); the vendor is fixed to the finding's; the **owner** starts on the vendor's assignee when that user belongs to the organization, else on the person adding the risk (`defaultActionOwner`), so the form can be saved as it opens; the risk level is the form's own, worked out from likelihood and severity as for any vendor risk (it can differ from the level chip on the card, which is the research's matrix: the form's level is the one saved); frameworks can be picked, and the custom fields of vendor risks are there, required ones included. A preview does not check the role again: the section only offers it to roles with `vendor.edit`.
- **Save** in the form calls `onSave`, which sends the accept request with the form's values (`toAcceptBody` in `application/utils/vendorResearchPreview.ts`: the owner as a number, the texts, the register's likelihood and severity, the level, the frameworks; no vendor). The risk is created and the finding settled in one transaction (see [Accept and dismiss](#accept-and-dismiss)). The form then saves the values of the custom fields on the new risk and closes, the row goes, and a toast ("Risk added to the register") and a notice with an **Open risk** button for the new risk are shown. If the custom field values cannot be saved, the form still closes (saving again would add the risk twice) and a warning says to open the risk and try again. **Cancel**, or closing the form any other way, changes nothing: the finding is still pending. A request that fails leaves the form open with everything that was typed, so that it can be saved again; the global error toast reports the failure.
- **Dismiss** opens `DismissFindingModal` (a `StandardModal`) with a required reason and an optional note of up to 500 characters, counted in code points as the server counts them (most emoji are one, a flag or a skin-tone emoji are two), and announces "Finding dismissed." when it is done.
- After a decision, focus moves to the first button of the row that takes the decided row's place: the next row, else the previous one, else the "Research findings" heading. Focus moves only if it has fallen to the page, so it never pulls a user back who has moved on.
- A decision that the server refuses is settled once the list has been read again. If the finding has left the pending list (404 for a finding or vendor that is gone, or 409 for a finding decided elsewhere), the dismiss modal or the vendor risk form of that finding closes by itself and focus moves as after a decision. This holds for the modal's finding whichever decision was answered last (two answers can arrive together, for example the refusal of one dismissal and the success of an accept of another finding), except while a request about that finding is still on its way: then its own answer decides. If the finding is still pending (the 409 of a lock held by another accept or the status job, `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT`), the modal stays open with the reason and the note, and a retry can succeed. The global error toast skips 404 on purpose, so a 404 on accept or dismiss shows the warning "This finding no longer exists." through `onNotify`.
- **Credibility.** Every finding shows its label under the caption "Credibility" (there is no confidence chip or fallback any more), neutral (it is not a risk colour), with its line under the row of ratings and, when a cited record matched by name only, the info line "Matched by name only: this may be another company." The line is worded from `credibility_basis` by `credibilityRationale` in `application/utils/vendorResearchText.ts`, in the person's language and in the server's English words, with the client's own names of the databases; the server's `credibility_rationale` is not shown. The client keeps its own copy of the ten lists that give context only, so that the line reads as the server's does: a finding that cites only records of them reads the sentence "Low: only registers that list the vendor are cited, and a register entry is not evidence of a risk." (translated), and such a record is left out of the sentence of a withdrawn record and of the day of a stale copy. A finding whose basis rule is `legacy_confidence` (a finding of a run from before the rules, until the backfill) shows its label, the server's sentence "Rated before the source rules; this is the earlier confidence level until the label is recomputed." and a muted "Earlier rating" note. Each source shows a small outlined badge of its category ("Official", "Trusted press", "Vendor", "Unverified") before its host, and a record that its database no longer stands by shows its status ("Withdrawn", "Rejected", "Delisted") after the date. A finding with no label, or with one the client does not know, shows no credibility block; a rule, category or status the client does not know shows nothing.
- With a vendor chosen, `RunSummary` shows the latest run: one line per category ("Security incidents: nothing found") with its note, the error of a failed run, the date of the last run, and **Research again** (`vendor.edit` only). While the run is queued or running it shows "Researching…" and the latest run is polled every 3 seconds. Under it, a run that has written its progress shows what it is doing ("Searching the web: Security incidents", "Reading a page: Certifications", "Analysing what was found", or "Starting the research" before the first turn), the categories researched so far ("Researched so far: ...") and the counts ("Searches: 3, pages read: 2"), all built from closed words and numbers by `describeProgress` (`application/utils/vendorResearchText.ts`), never from text of the web. The 2-minute polling window is counted from the latest sign of life: a run that keeps moving (its `progress.updatedAt` changes) is followed to its end however long it takes, and a read that finds that a run has moved after polling stopped starts polling again. A run that makes no progress for 2 minutes is given up on: the screen says "Still running. Check back later." followed by **Check again** (a text button, shown to every role), which reads the run at once and polls for another 2 minutes; and a run that is still queued or running is also read again when the browser tab regains focus (the app turns window-focus refetching off everywhere else). **Research again** is enabled again, so that the server decides. When a run that was seen active ends, the findings lists are refetched once.
- **Following a run.** A run started with the **Research risks** button on the vendors table is followed on the Risks tab. The button leaves a one-shot hint in the query cache under its own key (`["vendorResearchFollow"]`, so invalidating the research queries does not clear it), for a started run and for an "already running" answer, not for a refusal. The findings section reads the hint when it mounts and opens on that vendor, even with nothing pending, as if `findingsVendorId` were in the URL, so `RunSummary` polls and the findings appear when the run ends. The URL parameter wins when both are present, and the hint is cleared by the first mount either way. It is not time-bound: it stays until the section mounts or the query cache collects it (10 minutes after the latest start).
- The section stays mounted, with its state, while the register of vendor risks reloads after the **Risk status** or the project filter changes. Only the insights and the table give way to the skeleton.
- A run that is not active also shows one caption with its counters, "Searches: N, pages read: M" (from `searches_used` and `pages_read`), when they are not both zero, and one line that says which search it was (from `search_engine`): "Searched with Claude web search", or "News searched with the GDELT Project (last 3 months)" with "GDELT Project" as a link to the project, as the GDELT terms ask. A run with no engine (an older run, one that searched nothing, one none of whose searches was answered) shows no such line.
- A run shows its coverage after the category lines (`coverageLines` in `vendorResearchText.ts`): "Checked: …" with each list the run asked and its records (a read-through source, such as the NVD, is a list here, dated by its answer; a run stored before the NVD was read through shows it among the web databases), then the web databases that answered. A list added on 2026-11-10 (the 22 of `LISTS_SHOWN_WHEN_FOUND`: every one of them but Canada's sanctions list, which is named like the older lists) is named only when the run was offered at least one record of it, and the ones that were asked and held none are counted in one last item, "{count} more lists with no records" ("1 more list with no records"), or "{count} lists with no records" ("1 list with no records") when nothing else is named on the line, so that the line does not grow with every list that was asked; "Not checked: …" with each list that could not be checked (it had no copy yet, or its read failed), the added ones always named, and each web database that failed, so that a gap in the screening is never read as a clean result; "No records in … as of …" for the lists that held nothing, the 22 counted above not among them; "{list}: last updated {date}, not up to date" for each stale list it asked; "Sources cited: N official, N trusted press, N vendor, N unverified"; "Public databases turned off" when every list was off; and "Data sources: …", the attribution of each list it asked in its owner's words (of the 22 added on 2026-11-10, only of a list that offered a record, even if its read then failed, since a read-through list offers the records it had stored), which is never translated. The names and attributions are the client's own, by id; those stored in the coverage are not read. A run without coverage (a run from before the credibility rules) shows none of it.
- **Research settings.** An Admin in an organization that has an LLM key sees a **Research settings** button beside the vendor filter.
